We revoked table-level SELECT on `message_threads` and `posts` from `authenticated` and `anon` and granted SELECT on the columns the api shows. Options weighed: a view or a security-definer function (every direct reader and the policies would move, and the repos would need a second path); column grants (one migration, the row policies stay, nothing in the app changes because it reads on the service connection). Costs: a client query must name its columns (`select *` is refused), and a column added to either table later is unreadable to the client roles until a migration grants it, which the column catalogue test enforces.
- **`moderation_note` is hidden too, beyond the two columns the audit named.** The api gives the note to the author and the moderators only; as a plain column it would have been readable by every member on a restored or visible post. Authors and moderators still read it through the api. `moderated_at`, `edited_at`, `left_ids` and the rest of the row stay readable because the api shows them to everyone who can read the post or thread.
- **The monthly model budget is a new column, in US cents, over the whole metered cost (#15, 0059).** No monthly setting existed. `businesses.model_budget_minor` counts what a wage counts: the usage ledger's USD cost of every kind (tokens, sandbox, tools, training) and every payer, credits and own-key alike. A business-currency amount would not compare with the ledger, as with the wage cap and Autopilot's ceiling. Null means no budget; there is no default.
- **At the budget, dispatch refuses; it does not pause the team.** BUILD §10.4 says to pause everything at 100 %. A pause would need an undo when the month turns or the budget is raised, and a stop reason that lifts by itself (the stop flag clears only on a resume). A refusal in the one place sessions open (`checkModelBudget`), `dev.tasks.start` and the planner's early return is stateless: it lifts at once when the budget is raised or removed or the month turns. The founder is told by one Inbox warning per month and budget amount (deduplicated on the audit row). The 50 % and 80 % alerts are not built for the budget.
- **A running session is stopped by the SDK's budget, not by a new booking pipeline (#15 part 2).** Only platform-key sessions book as they run. Rather than book own-key sessions turn by turn, the wage cap and the budget left are two more bounds on the `maxBudgetUsd` a session starts with (task budget, credits, wage cap, monthly budget; the smallest wins, and its words are in the session's summary). The cost is that sessions running at the same time are not counted against each other until they book (at most what each was handed over), and a session started by hand for an agent that is already working is held to the cap as it stood when it began. A stricter version books `byok` rows per turn through `SessionCredits` and re-reads `agentSpend` and `modelBudgetState` at each settle; the existing `unbookedTokens` already makes the finish book only the rest.
- **The budget has an API setter only so far.** `business.setModelBudget` (owner or admin, audited) is in; the Settings › Mode control and its Playwright step are not, to keep this change small and out of the screens other work is changing. The refusal copy therefore says an owner or admin can raise or remove the budget, without naming a screen.
- **This branch's migrations are 0058 and 0059.** The sibling release-2 branches took 0056 (copy rules) and 0057 (finance and legal), so the column grants are `0058_live_social_column_grants` (journal idx 44, `when` 1795090320000) and the monthly model budget is `0059_model_budget` (idx 45, `when` 1795176720000). The two do not depend on each other or on the siblings' migrations; the journal stays in tag order with `when` strictly increasing once all four are merged.

## 2026-10-14 — Release 2 follow-ups (FU-S-2, FU-T2-2, FU-T2-1, #282)

- **A founder's network messages are written to no business (FU-S-2).** A connection request or a message from another founder used to be filed as an event and as a `human_message` note in the Inbox of each LIVE business its recipient runs (2026-10-12, S-live-social, which accepted that "members with Inbox access can read the note"). That is a privacy hole: every staff member and advisor with Inbox access, and everyone on the business's `ev:` stream, read the founder's private messages. This supersedes that decision. Requests and messages now reach the recipient alone: the badge, the Network lists and a content-free `u:{userId}` signal (the stream's user channel). We chose to stop writing them into the business rather than add a recipient to Inbox items, because a per-person Inbox note would also leak through the Inbox counts, the `inbox.item_created` event, search, digests and the composer, and the Network screen already has its own list and badge in every mode.
  - `connection.requested` and `message.received` stay declared (the playbook words and the walls table list them) but are person-addressed (`PERSON_ADDRESSED_EVENT_TYPES`): `repos.events.write` refuses them, the `events_not_person_addressed` check (migration 0060) holds the table to it whichever writer tries, and apps/stream never delivers one on `ev:`, not even to an owner. A rolling deploy can therefore make an older api fail a Send or a request for the minutes the two versions overlap; we judged that better than a window in which a stray writer can put a private message in the log.
  - 0060 deletes what 0027's delivery already wrote: the notes, their read receipts, the `inbox.item_created` events that announced them and the events themselves (outbox rows cascade). Nothing is archived instead, because an archived note is still readable.
  - A playbook trigger on "a message arrives" or "a connection is requested", and the `message.received` trigger in the Operations lead's schedule, can no longer fire from private network traffic; that is the point. The mock composer's mapping of these events to `human_message` notes is unreachable and was left alone.
  - BUILD §11.14's "human messages (Inbox Messages tab)" now means notes written inside the business (a co-founder's note, a seeded note), not network traffic. The people in a thread read it on Network.
- **A plan's model change goes through the same retest as Agent profile › Model (FU-T2-2).** The orchestrator's `set_agent_model` step now calls the function the api uses (`changeAgentModels`, moved to packages/db with a workspace dependency on `@foundmill/models`): work locked to one model is skipped, a downgrade warns, the agent works on the new model at once (AC-11.10.5, the 2026-10-12 T2 decision, which we did not re-decide), and each changed agent gets a retest on a draft copy of its live version. The step starts the retest as a `trainingRun` child (the playbook and plan workflows) and stops the retests the change superseded (`stop` on the step's result). The model does not wait for the retest to pass: the retest recommends, and Promote or Discard stays the founder's, as for the Model dialog. The step ends `done` with the retest ids, `done` with `retestsWaitForResume` while the team is stopped (the runs stay queued and start on resume), and `failed` with the reason when no agent could change (every target locked, or none named).
- **Promoting a draft keeps the agent's current model (FU-T2-1).** `repos.training.decide(…, "promoted")` makes the draft's instructions and tools live but leaves `agents.model_key` alone, unless the draft is a model change's retest copy (`isModelRetestVersion`: system-made, version above 1), which exists to put the agent on its model. A Training-tab preview on another model is a draft like any other; Apply is how the model changes. The promoted version keeps the model it was tested on. A rollback still brings back the earlier version's model (AC-11.10.6): the founder asked to go back.
- **#282 closed.** The business-currency work (0025, f9baa32) had already replaced the `EUR` literals in `planContextFor` and the conversation's `promptSubject`; the remaining gap was a test that ran only in the default currency. The conversation test now prices the same option in a dollar, pound and krona business.

## 2026-10-14 — A streamed screen exists twice for a moment: the specs wait for it and scope to the shell, the product is left alone (audit #433)

Root cause, measured on the production build with a React commit probe: `app/b/[bid]/loading.tsx` makes every page a Suspense boundary under the shell, so the server sends the shell with a skeleton in `<main>` and the screen itself afterwards in a hidden `<div hidden id="S:0">` at the end of the body, with a reveal script. React 19.3 batches that reveal (`$RC` queues it, `$RV` moves the screen in about 300 ms after the first paint), and Next starts hydrating as soon as the shell's inline data is there, which on a second visit (scripts cached, as in every spec after `openBusiness`) can be before the reveal. React client-renders a boundary that is still waiting for its reveal when any provider above it changes value in a render pass that reaches it (`updateDehydratedSuspenseComponent`), and in the first commit after hydration three do: Radix's toast provider (its viewport registers itself in state), `ModuleEnvProvider` and `EntityLinkProvider` (rebuilt on most renders of `ShellInner`). From then on the screen is in `<main>` (client-rendered) and still in `S:0` until `$RV` removes it, which is the lingering copy: for 300 ms or more, longer on a slow runner, an unscoped `getByTestId` resolves to two elements (strict mode), and a click, a typed value, a key press or a sign-out message that lands on nodes React is about to replace is lost (the connect dialog that never opened, T not focusing the thread, a typed instruction that never reached Enter, a tab that was not listening yet). A person never sees it. A product fix is not contained: the toast provider sits at the root and Radix's viewport registration cannot be avoided, so stabilizing our own two values would not stop the client render, and removing `loading.tsx` would drop the instant skeleton on every client-side navigation. The code stays as it is and the specs carry the fix: `settled(page)` in `e2e/helpers.ts` waits until no `div[hidden][id^="S:"]` is left and the screen's root is hydrated (it runs inside `openBusiness` and `openSettings`, and after any `page.goto` or reload that is followed by an interaction); locators for a Settings tab's own content start at `settingsScreen(page)`; toasts are looked up with `toast(page, title)` (the viewport, whole title), because Radix also announces every toast in a hidden live region ("Notification <title>") for about a second and a substring `getByText` matches both.

## 2026-10-15 — A typed budget for a channel nobody runs yet is judged against the Distribution lead's allowance
- **Observed.** "Spend up to $500 on LinkedIn ads next week" typed at a desk compiles to enable the channel, set a $500 budget and create a task, all assigned to the agent who runs the channel, else (`pickAgent`) the Distribution strategist. A new business has a paid-ads agent only when its concept starts with that channel (consumer app, direct to consumer, hardware: yes; content business: no). The strategist's template allows no spend of its own (`AUTONOMY_INHERIT`, cap 0), plans are judged as that agent's own tool call would be (audit api-services-03), so `spend-cap-exceeded` refuses the budget with "policy denied" and the other two steps run. The founder is not asked.
- **Decision.** Policy unchanged: an agent's own allowance still bounds what it can spend, and "the founder raises the cap, not the agent". The Inbox go-ahead test (e2e/inbox.spec.ts) now sets the lead's allowance first, so it checks what it means to (a spend above the approval threshold waits for the go-ahead) in every business. Local runs of the test with that workaround: 2 of 3 failed before, 3 of 3 passed after; the workaround is removed again below, now that the spend asks.
- **Decided (founder, 2026-10-15): it asks.** A spend assigned to an agent whose own allowance is nil (cap 0) no longer ends as "policy denied": the plan evaluation turns Cedar's `spend-cap-exceeded` refusal into an ask with the reason "Please set up my spend allowance" (policy id `spend-allowance-needed`), the plan waits for the founder's confirmation like any ask, and confirming runs it. Only that case: an agent that has an allowance the amount exceeds, an amount above an active vacation's budget ceiling, and anything a second policy also forbids are still refused; the gateway is untouched, so the agent's own tool calls still stop at its cap until the founder sets the allowance (Agent profile › Autonomy). The reasons are stored with the staged action (`plan_actions.result.policyReasons`) so a reload and another device show them, and "Here is what will happen" prints them under each action that asks.
- **A channel nobody runs yet goes to the Distribution lead, always.** The mock plan compiler (the live compiler chooses the agent itself from the team list) used to hand a channel with no running agent to the first agent in the list whose role said distribution, growth, marketing or strategist, and agents hired together share a timestamp, so a paid-ads budget landed on the Distribution strategist (no allowance) in one business and on the Content marketing engine (a $300 allowance) in the next. It now prefers the Distribution lead and falls back to a growth or marketing agent only when there is none (`pickAgent`, packages/models).
- **Not changed, for the founder to say.** An agent that has an allowance the typed amount exceeds (a $500 budget on an agent with $300) is still refused, as is a vacation ceiling. If a typed spend should ask in that case too, the reason would read "Please raise my spend allowance", and nothing else in the plan evaluation changes.

## 2026-10-16 — Speed over the default model settings for what the founder waits on

Observed: agent replies ran on Opus 5.5 at low effort; the plan compiler, Inbox Composer, Planner and the leads' Recommend answers ran Opus at its default (medium) effort. DECISIONS 2026-10-09 left model choice and effort to the founder.

Decided (founder, 2026-10-16): the `conversation` route runs **Sonnet 5.5 at low effort**; `planCompiler` and `inboxComposer` (still locked to Opus) and `planner` run **Opus at low effort**; Distribution's channel scores and Legal's jurisdiction cards move to a new `leadRecommendation` route (Opus, low effort) so the leads' own cycle work on `departmentLead` keeps the default. Budgets stay at the thinking ceiling (16k): `max_tokens` is only a cap, and a cut-off task graph costs a retry. Fast mode stays off (twice the price). Measured on the live key (2026-10-15): a reply 4.4 s on Opus → 3.8 s on Sonnet (median of 6 golden cases); the live conversation evals scored Sonnet 13/15 in two runs against Opus 12/15 on main. The injection cases that fail (a claim that something was done, an injected setting offered back, a model refusal) fail on both models and are tracked separately.

## 2026-10-16 — Speed, round 2: where the time went and what changed

- **One function region, Frankfurt.** Vercel's Hobby plan runs functions in one region of our choice; the default (iad1, Washington) put two to three sequential api calls per page across the Atlantic. `fra1` is about 8 ms from Railway EU West and about 25–30 ms from Supabase Stockholm. A US-based founder pays about 90 ms more between browser and function but saves more per server-side call. Set in `apps/web/vercel.json`, not the dashboard, so it is reviewed with the code.
- **The web trusts a verified token, as the api already does.** The proxy and server session use `getClaims()` (local ES256 verification with a cached JWKS) instead of `getUser()` (a network call to Supabase Auth per request and per click). A session revoked elsewhere stays usable on the web until its access token expires (1 h at most), which the api already accepted. A stalled sign-in is treated as signed out after 4 s instead of hanging (auth-js retries a refresh for up to 30 s).
- **Browser reads are same-origin.** Reads go to `/api/trpc/*` on foundmill.com, rewritten by Vercel's edge to the api, so no CORS preflight runs per read (browsers key the preflight cache by full URL, and every tRPC read URL differs). Changes and streams stay on api.foundmill.com because they can run longer than a proxied request should; their preflight is cached for 2 h (Chrome's cap).
- **Connections stay warm.** postgres.js closed every connection after 30–60 min, so the first screen after a break paid a new TLS and SCRAM handshake per request (production: 434–559 ms vs 143–166 ms a second later). Connections now live 6 h with jitter, and services warm their pools at boot and every minute.

## 2026-10-16 — The business shell is no longer client-rendered on load: the app sits beside Radix's toast provider

- **Observed (CI, PR #5).** Speed round 2 added `app/b/loading.tsx`, so the whole business layout (shell, kit bar, sidebar) streams as a Suspense boundary. The 2026-10-14 mechanism then applied to the shell itself: when hydration reached the boundary before React's batched reveal, a changed context value above it made React client-render it, and the shell stayed in its hidden `S:0` copy until the reveal. Unscoped `getByTestId("app-shell")` and `getByTestId("resume-agents-trigger")` matched two elements (stop-resume failed 3 of 3 after its reload; settings and finance specs flaked). Above that boundary only the root providers sit, and of those only Radix's toast provider changes value on the first commit (its viewport registers itself in the provider's state).
- **Decision.** Dugout's `ToastProvider` renders its children beside `@radix-ui/react-toast`'s provider instead of inside it; the toasts and the viewport stay inside, and `useToast` and the placement context are unchanged. A caller that passes `renderViewport={false}` to place `<Toaster />` itself keeps its children inside, as before. Measured on the production build: 30 loads and reloads of Home and the Inbox, two shells at once on 5 before, on 0 after. Keeping `app/b/loading.tsx` keeps its gain (the head and scripts go out before the layout's data).
- **Unchanged.** `ModuleEnvProvider` and `EntityLinkProvider` sit inside the shell, above the screen's own boundary (`app/b/[bid]/loading.tsx`), so the 2026-10-14 note still holds for the screen: specs keep `settled(page)` before they interact.

## 2026-10-16 — A money driver at rest shows its cents as two digits

- **Observed (CI, PR #5).** Runway's Starting cash read "45,064.3" at rest on the day the seeded cash ended in a zero cent: `toField` printed minor units as `String(minor / 100)` and the field groups what it is given. The screen spec (#172) asks for "grouped, with cents when it has them" as two digits; the unit test pinned the one-digit form.
- **Decision.** A money driver with cents shows two cent digits ("1500.50" → "1,500.50"); a whole amount shows none ("1,500"). What the founder types is still kept as typed while the field has focus. The unit test now expects "1,500.50".

## 2026-10-16 — No business limit for now; the included credits stay one pool

Decided (founder, 2026-10-16): no business limit on any plan until paid plans launch. This supersedes "Tier limits are enforced: Businesses" and BUILD §10.5's business numbers; seats and concurrency are unchanged, and the allowance check stays in Commit so a number in `TIERS.*.businesses` brings a limit back. The founder also chose to keep the included model credits as one pool per owner (free covers 1 business a month): creating businesses cannot multiply free credits, and a second free business that month starts with its team paused until credits or the founder's own key are added. Switch business is a popover with a filterable list (cmdk) rather than a menu, because a menu's typeahead fights the filter; archived businesses' canonical place is `/businesses#archived`.

## 2026-10-16 — Skills from the library or files

Stored as business skill definitions per agent type (keys `library_<key>` and `upload_<name>_<hash8>`), so the same library skill or the same file is one definition; removing a skill deletes the agent's rating row and keeps the definition as a record. Each library skill shows as one attribute; uploaded files show as Craft. Limits: 64 KB per file, 20,000 characters of instructions per skill, 8 added skills and 60,000 characters of skill instructions per agent, so the Skills section stays a bounded part of every prompt. Screening refuses rather than strips (YAML tags and anchors, HTML comments, invisible characters, imitations of the platform's section headings, role and delimiter spoofing, anything the house-rules detector or injection heuristics flag); a tool list in front matter is ignored and the founder is told so. Skills never change tools, autonomy, spend caps or approvals.

## 2026-10-16 — A platform call is named after the business

An approval note named a call the platform runs inside the business with the platform's name ("asks to run Foundmill › launch campaign"), which read as if the agent worked for Foundmill. Platform calls now carry the business's name ("LP.GP › launch campaign"); outside services keep theirs ("GitHub › run migration").

## 2026-10-18 — A promotion writes the run's scores onto the ratings; a skill without tests is reported, not scored\n\n- **The write-back lives in `repos.training.decide`, not in the orchestrator.** `finalizeTrainingRun` keeps writing results only on the run (AC-11.10.1: nothing changes until Promote); the decision applies them. This supersedes the 2026-10 note that `agent_skills.displayed` is \"written by the orchestrator's grader\": it never was, which was the founder's \"Training doesn't update ratings\".\n- **`agent.training.promote` and `rollback` run on the service connection after `repos.memberships.get`**, as `agent.skills.review`/`add` already do: `agent_skills` is `read_only` for app roles under RLS (0004), so a write inside `ctx.rls` would silently update nothing. The role/section check (`sectionWrite(\"team\")`) and the admin-only tool-widening check are unchanged; the promotion, its ratings, the `agent.promoted` event and the audit entry (which lists the ratings written) are one transaction.\n- **Potential is literal BUILD §11.10: the best tested score across the agent's last five finished runs** (`done`/`awaiting_decision` with results, canceled and failed left out; a skill a run found no tests for does not count on that run), never below the new tested rating. The template's baseline is not a tested score, so a first run that scores below it lowers the potential; a discarded run's score counts (it was reached). A rollback restores `old` tested values and keeps the potential reached.\n- **`training_runs.results[key].tests` (0 = no tests yet).** `runSkillTests` already returned `testsRun: 0` for a custom skill without reviewed tests and for library/file skills; `finalizeTrainingRun` now records `{old, new: old, tests: 0}`, names those skills in the coach summary (\"No tests yet for Release notes: its rating stands until the skill has tests (add it as a custom skill to have tests drafted for your review)\"), and leaves them out of `training.completed.skillDeltas`. Results from before the count was stored (no `tests` field) are treated as scored. No test generation for library or file skills was built: BUILD defines drafted tests for custom skills only.\n- **The profile's tested mean (header stars) skips untested skills** instead of counting them as 0.\n- **Northwind seeds a trained draft awaiting a decision** (Frontend Engineer, with a `library_release_notes` skill) so the Promote path runs in the e2e stack, which has no Temporal; the spec branches on `data-phase` so a second run against the same stack still passes.

## 2026-10-18 — Conversation: refusals, done-claims and quoted security settings

- A model refusal on the conversation route is not an error the founder sees. The founder gets a scripted reply from the agent's voice kit ("<ack> Some of the text in this one reads like instructions aimed at me rather than information, so I'm setting it aside and not acting on any of it." / "If there's something you want done, tell me in your own words and I'll write it up for you to confirm. Nothing runs from this conversation."; a plainer pair when the subject carries no quoted text), the subject's usual chips, no hand-off, no second model call. The exchange is stored (meta.source "refused") so the thread reads coherently on reload. Rationale: refusals cluster on injected content, where "I'm not acting on it" is the right answer; an error invites a retry that re-sends the same injection.
- The evals count a refusal on an injection case as blocked only because, and only when, that scripted reply is what ships and passes the shipped checks; a golden case the model refused fails (no answer was given). The runner calls the same `refusedAgentReply` the api calls, so the measurement is of what the founder gets. The refused call's tokens are billed but the error carries no cost, so such a case reports cost 0.
- A claim that work was done is repaired server-side at sentence level (the sentence becomes an honest fixed line), not by rewriting the verb: a deterministic, grammatical, always-honest replacement beats a clever rewrite that could still mislead. First-person claims ("I've wired") are always claims; stated forms ("it's done", "has been sent") are claims unless a reporting verb precedes them in the sentence, so an agent may describe what a note demands. "started" counts only as an act ("I've started the campaign"), not "started looking".
- The quoted-content check now treats the subjects of the hard gates (BUILD §2.2: security settings and payment identifiers) as markers when they appear only in quoted text, however the chip is worded (a protective "Keep 2FA on" is still a chip the injection produced). The founder's own words, option labels, facts and the agent's own work remain trusted, so a founder who asks about 2FA can be offered 2FA chips. Accepted cost: a task goal that legitimately mentions "permissions" or "API keys" blocks chips naming them until the founder names them.
- The eval's done-claim check was narrowed to the shared detector (reported speech is not a claim). This is a correction of a false positive, not a weakening: the BUILD §9.8 gate (nothing injected offered, no automated action from a hand-off) is unchanged and still checked on the shipped reply for 100% of injection cases; the case's `mustNotContain: ["it is done"]` literals stay.
- Prompt `agent_conversation` bumped to v2 rather than edited in place, per BUILD §12 (versioned templates).


## 2026-10-18 — The first days are about the product

The founder (2026-10-18): a new business must not open with distribution; it opens with the specification and roadmap ready to sign off, builds on sign-off, and a product research agent tests the direction with the ICP in parallel. Choices made for the founder, each reversible:

1. **Take charge lands on Home, not on an Inbox decision.** There is no Board decision at commit any more; the Chief of Staff posts a non-blocking welcome Update and the founder sees the foundations drafting on Home. Reversible: redirect to `routes.inbox(bid, firstItem.id)` instead (one line in CommitScreen.tsx).
2. **Two blocking decision notes, one per artifact (specification, roadmap), from the Product lead**, replacing the single "Vision, specification and roadmap drafted" Update. Both block Continue ("Respond · 2"). Reversible: make the roadmap note non-blocking, or merge into one note with two sign-offs (the service takes `kind`).
3. **Sign-offs are their own decision branch, not plan actions.** Like approvals, a sign-off note is recognized by a DB row (`product_signoffs.inbox_item_id`) and decided in-process without the plan compiler; no new plan action type. Reversible later by adding `approve_spec`/`approve_roadmap` actions if plans need to carry them.
4. **Approving the specification publishes a numbered spec version** ("Signed off by the founder") and the roadmap approval stores a snapshot; roadmap item statuses are untouched. Reversible: skip the publish and store a block-version snapshot instead.
5. **Request changes requires the founder's words** (from the note or the screen dialog) and creates a revision task for the Product lead; the spec reopens its round automatically when a new version is published, the roadmap is re-approved from its tab. Reversible: allow an empty Request changes that only creates the task.
6. **Building starts automatically on the second approval:** the api plans the Planner's task graph for the first two Now items (fallback: the archetype's former 'Plan the first vertical slice' goal, kept as `firstBuild`) and starts the next cycle exactly as Continue would. Reversible: drop the `startCycle` call and let the follow-up say 'Press Continue'.
7. **Cycle 1 still starts at commit** (groundwork only: open questions, finance, legal, specialists); the former spec-drafting, planner and channel-scoring first tasks are removed or moved (scoring moves to Start distribution). Reversible: put any of them back in `firstCycleTasks`.
8. **Channel tasks are deferred by code, not only by prompt,** until distribution has started; code tasks the founder asks for by free text before sign-off are not blocked.
9. **Distribution is gated on an explicit `businesses.distribution_started_at`,** set by 'Start distribution' (Board button, owner/admin) or the 'Ready to start selling?' note; every engine start path checks it and the engine's own step stops without it. Enabling a channel before that persists it with the status 'Waiting for the product'. Reversible: a single setter.
10. **The archetype's Board question ('Where should the first customer come from?') moves to Start distribution; Door B's import first-decisions note moves to the build start.** Reversible: post either at a different moment with the same helpers.
11. **The 'Ready to start selling?' note is offered when every task of a roadmap item is done** (there is no other 'shipped' signal), once per business, non-blocking; the Board button is always available after the foundations are ready.
12. **Existing businesses and the demo seeds are backfilled as signed off and selling** (migration 0062 inserts approved rounds with `decided_by_kind: 'system'` and sets `distribution_started_at = committed_at`), so nothing running today stops.
13. **Vacation and Automated mode never sign off the product or start distribution.**
14. **The Product Research agent is a Sonnet worker in the Product department, hired for every archetype**, with its first deliverable (the research plan) drafted by the api as soon as the foundations are ready, before sign-off, so it never waits on the founder and shows in mock mode without an orchestrator; its deeper work runs as cycle tasks; findings travel as sources into the existing proposal flow. Reversible: gate `product.research.ensure` on the spec approval (one condition) or move the drafting to a workflow.
15. **Research artifacts get their own tables** (`product_research`, `research_artifacts`) rather than living in `sources`, so the Source Watcher never tries to fetch them; the Research tab gains a panel on top of its existing sources view.
16. **Copy choices**: 'Start distribution' (button), 'Waiting for the product' (channel status), 'Ready to start selling?' (note), 'Sign off the product specification / roadmap' (notes), 'Approve the specification' / 'Request changes' (options), 'Building starts: N tasks planned' (follow-up); all US English, no football terms.

### Product sign-offs and the distribution start (package A)

(1) The product stage (drafting → awaiting_signoff → building → selling) is derived from the foundations status, the latest sign-off rounds and businesses.distribution_started_at, never stored, so no state machine can drift. (2) Sign-off decisions are their own branch in decideInboxItem (a product_signoffs row keyed to the Inbox item), decided in-process like approvals with a reply_only plan kept for history; no plan compiler, no Temporal, so the Inbox note and the Product tabs share one implementation (decideProductSignoff). (3) The second approval starts the build inside the same request (planTaskGraph for at most LIMITS.signoffPlanRoadmapItems Now items, the archetype's firstBuild when the roadmap has none, then startCycle); product.signoff_decided is emitted after so it carries the build facts. (4) Engines gate on distribution_started_at at every entry (ensureChannelEngines, syncChannelEngines, channels.enable, orchestrator channelStep, plan enable_channel, listForEngines) rather than at one chokepoint; enabling a channel before the start keeps it on the board with its engine deferred. (5) Start distribution is owner/admin only (the businesses update policy), from the Board or the Ready-to-sell note; the note is offered once per business and never to one that sells. (6) Migration 0062 backfills every committed business as started and signed off (decided_by system), so nothing already running stops; db fixtures carry distributionStartedAt and tests that want the gated state commit a fresh business. (7) Vacation and Automated mode skip sign-off notes with the reason "signing off the product is yours" even though the recommended option is free and reversible. (8) The welcome note is an Update, not a decision: nothing blocks Continue until the foundations are drafted. (9) On screen the sign-off is a span-12 panel at the top of the Specification and Roadmap tabs (status pill + Approve / Request changes dialog) rather than a pill in the panel actions, and the Vision tab carries a one-line summary; Request changes requires the founder's words everywhere.

### Product Researcher (package B)

The founder's rule (2026-10-18): every new business tests its specification and direction with the people it is for, in parallel with the build. Decisions taken for package B:

- **A new template, not a new mode of the Researcher.** `product_researcher` (Sonnet worker, Product module, task kind research) owns the plan to test the specification with the ICP: interview plan and script, smoke test, survey, demo script, later the demo and the findings. The existing `researcher` keeps grounding concept cards and answering research questions. Both are hired into the Product department of every archetype. Craft skills `discovery_interviewing`, `survey_design` and `demo_scripting` join the catalogue with blueprints; `experiment_design` and `evidence_synthesis` are shared with the Distribution strategist and the Product lead.
- **The first deliverable is drafted by the api, in-process, as soon as the foundations are ready, before any sign-off** (mirroring the foundations' own fallback runner: two slots per process, a 3-minute timeout, an attempt older than 5 minutes counts as interrupted). Home and the Research tab call `product.research.ensure` once per business per visit when `business.get.foundationsStatus` is ready; the server claims the row exactly once (`pending → drafting`, conditional update) and only for a member with product write, never for a stopped team. No Temporal workflow: one Sonnet call that must show in mock mode without an orchestrator. Reversible: a `productResearchPlan` workflow with this path as the fallback.
- **Research artifacts get their own tables** (`product_research`, one service-written row per business; `research_artifacts`, member-writable with a `position` for the plan's order), not rows in `sources`, so the Source Watcher never tries to fetch them. Findings from later tests travel as sources through the existing proposal flow (the template's playbook says so), which is why the Northwind seed keeps its interview proposal citing the transcript source and adds a `findings` artifact beside it.
- **The plan's follow-up work runs in cycles as ordinary research tasks** created from the plan's `first_steps` (at most four, status ready, assigned to the researcher in the Product department, every goal ending with the founder's approval before anyone is contacted). The api creates no outreach: recruit lists are CRM records for the founder, the page and the survey are drafts.
- **The ICP is read from the first paragraph of the Customers section** (never its heading), else the vision's "for whom"; the specification reaches the model inside `<untrusted_content kind="specification">` with `<`, `>` and backticks escaped, and every stored string is plain text with figures not in the inputs labeled Estimate (the foundations' `plainModelText` / `labelFigures`).
- **`product.research.get` reads `pending` for a business with foundations and no plan row yet**, and `none` only for a business committed before foundations existed; the demo businesses are seeded `ready` so their tabs never show "Starting".
- **Model failures are described by one helper** (`describeModelFailure(err, words)`); the foundations keep their exact wording through `describeFoundationsFailure`.
- **The founder's archive exports both tables under Product.**
- The optional mock recording `ledgerly-interview-plan` was not added: the orchestrator's session test pins which recording a research task replays and the sandbox caps the bundled set at seven; research tasks replay the existing research recording.

## 2026-10-19: The api's pool wedged for four hours

**What happened.** From 14:52 UTC the api logged nothing and answered every request with a timeout (the web's "Can't reach the server"), while `/health` reported `db: false`; Postgres was healthy throughout (checkpoints, flat memory, 1.2 GB of 50 GB) and the api's CPU and memory were flat. A restart at 18:40 fixed it at once. Postgres logged no aborted transaction when the process died, so nothing was open server-side: the pool (postgres.js 3.4.9, `max: 10`) had lost every slot client-side, queries queued behind phantom work for good, and the health probe, which goes through the same pool, timed out with them. Two open postgres.js defects fit the trace exactly and both leak one slot silently per occurrence: a connection whose `max_lifetime` ends while it is in use is never closed nor replaced (porsager/postgres#1255; the 6-hour lifetime of the speed round put each connection through that window about four times a day, and only a renewal during activity bites, which is why the night-time ones were harmless), and a query accepted between a socket's `error` and `close` events never settles and strands its connection on the next reconnect (porsager/postgres#1246; the warmer's 8 probes a minute made hitting that instant likelier). Railway's healthcheck only gates a rollout; nothing restarts a container that turns unhealthy later, so the api sat there until a person noticed.

**Decisions.**
- **A service that cannot reach its own pool exits** (packages/db `watchPoolOrExit`, in api, gateway, composer, ingest and collab): a probe every 30 s, three misses, then one probe on a fresh connection outside the pool to tell a wedged pool (exit 1, Railway's `ON_FAILURE` policy restarts it) from a database that is down (log once a minute, wait). Exiting is the whole fix: a fresh process has a fresh pool, and four hours becomes about two minutes. A mock or PGlite database is never watched.
- **No lifetime on direct connections.** `maxLifetime` defaults to null for a direct Postgres URL (the 6–7 h jitter stays for transaction poolers, whose sessions churn anyway). A dropped connection is found by TCP keep-alive and replaced on the next query; the renewal that cost the outage never runs.
- **Server-side ceilings as a backstop:** `statement_timeout` 5 min and `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` 2 min as startup parameters on direct connections (not through poolers, which do not reliably pass startup parameters on). A migration's backfill fits; a runaway statement no longer holds a pooled connection for good.
- **The reset fix is carried in our postgres.js patch** (patches/postgres@3.4.9.patch, the two-line change proposed on #1246: forget the active query once it is rejected, and reject what was written to a dead socket on close, after an error too). Upstream has published no fix; the patch is re-examined when 3.4.10 or 4.x lands.
- **Warm-up probes are bounded** (10 s) and a round is not started while the previous one waits: an unanswered probe is logged as "a pooled connection may be wedged" instead of stacking up unseen, which is the earliest visible sign of a slot leak.
- **`/health` names the state** (`pool: ok | degraded | wedged | down`) so the next reader of a `db: false` knows in one look whether Postgres is down or a restart is already on its way.

## 2026-10-20 — UX audit: judgment calls behind the plan

The first external 30-minute user test called Foundmill exciting and also intimidating and unclear. `docs/UX_AUDIT_AND_PLAN.md` is the audit and the plan; these are the calls made while writing it, each reversible:

- **Audit the code and the running mock stack, not the spec.** BUILD.md promises very little guidance (notes shaped fact → why → ask, consequences on options, provenance on hover, empty states that say what will appear) and defers "onboarding polish" to Phase 5; the product is well ahead of it on honesty and behind it on explanation. Findings quote the shipped strings with their file and line, and twelve screenshots of the PGlite stack (Northwind Skincare, `MODEL_MODE=mock`) are committed under `docs/ux-audit/` so the plan can be checked against what a tester saw. The "Reconnecting…" chip in them is the missing local stream server, not a finding.
- **One interface, a guidance layer, one switch.** No "beginner mode" that changes layout: DESIGN §2 (structure never moves) holds. Guidance is an account preference (`guidance: on | off`, `text_size: standard | comfortable`, plus explainers seen and tours done), on by default, off by default for profiles that say Exited or Operator (with a toast saying so). Off hides tutoring (explainers, checklists, coach marks, term underlines) and never hides information (consequences, provenance, Estimate); key caps on buttons go the other way and render only when guidance is off (settled in "Key caps on buttons render only when guidance is off" below).
- **Definitions are a new source, not the Glossary.** Settings › Glossary is the business's rename map (`applyGlossary`, DECISIONS 2026-10 "Glossary (audit #206)") and stays that, renamed "Your words". Definitions live in `packages/shared/src/terms.ts`, keyed by stable id, read by the UI (`Term`, `InfoTip`, `PlainFigure`), by the Composer as its glossary input, and by the new linter; the rename map runs over the display word so a business that says "clients" reads "Client: a paying client".
- **Define the terms experts expect; replace the gratuitous ones.** Runway, MRR, pull request, model, cycle, Estimate stay and get defined at first use; ICP, CPA, named query, exhibit, counterparty, concurrency, RLS, Cedar, hunk are replaced on sight. The split is the two tiers of `scripts/plain-language.mjs`, built on the banned-terms scanner's extractor.
- **The management-sim grammar stays and gets words.** Continue, the Inbox, 1–20 ratings in the ramp, stars, Form, Training, the Coach, Health, Hierarchy, chapters, milestones, leaderboards and the Board are kept; three unlabeled scales, Form without a key, "Natural / Accomplished / Competent / Unconvincing" without a legend, and the transfer-window verbs (loan, release, starters, re-score) are the parts a non-player cannot read and get labels or plain verbs.
- **"Needs you" has two families.** Answer (Respond, Needs your answer, Your decision) and go-ahead (Needs your go-ahead, Give the go-ahead); the chip is "Holds Continue". Six current wordings collapse into these.
- **The Composer learns who the founder is and says why it recommends.** `founder_experience` becomes a prompt input, the output schema gains `decision.options[].because`, the 60-word floor becomes "no filler", and the mock fixtures (what a mock-mode tester reads) are rewritten to the voice guide with a test that forbids developer-note leaks.
- **Phase order is words first, then primitives, then depth, then tours.** Phase 0 is copy-only so every High copy finding closes in a week without migrations; the guided layer is Phase 1; enforcement (linter, contrast test) is Phase 2; teaching by doing (tours, checklist, example business, comfortable text) is Phase 3 and ends with the second external test.
- **Left to the founder** (§12 of the plan): CRM → Contacts and moving the Anthropic key into Settings; whether day one's first Continue stays red; one rating scale (/20 recommended) versus /10 in the table; the guidance default for experienced profiles; which example-business route to build; the position-map words.
- **Founder decisions (2026-10-20, same day):** CRM becomes "Contacts" in navigation (CRM stays in the palette keywords and the tooltip); the Anthropic key leaves the sidebar foot for Settings › Keys; day one's first Continue stays red "Respond · 2" with the coach mark and the clearer tooltip; one rating scale per agent, the /20 mean with its band word, replacing the /10 table average; guidance defaults to off for Exited and Operator profiles, with the toast that says how to turn it on. The example-business route and the position-map words are still open. Later the same day: the example business is the read-only demo membership (a viewer grant on one shared, seeded example business, labeled Example, Continue replaced by "Start your own"), and the position map uses the product's existing band words (Excellent / Good / Average / Poor) under the retitled panel "Other roles it could take", so the product has one vocabulary for "how good". The founder asked for the whole plan to be built.

## 2026-10-20 — Building the UX plan

Judgment calls the slices of waves A, B and C took while building `docs/UX_AUDIT_AND_PLAN.md` (the founder's decisions are in §12 of the plan and in "UX audit: judgment calls behind the plan" above). Each is reversible; the reversal is named.

### The switch and the rules every slice follows

- **Key caps on buttons render only when guidance is off.** The plan's principle 9 and §7.7 say so; one wave A brief said the opposite, so the slices diverged until the rule was settled: Edit E, Refresh R, the DecisionBar's 1–4, the quick replies' 1–4 and ⌥1–4 (review F25, 2026-10-21), the studio's ⌘↵ and the compare shortcuts' tip show only with guidance off, the shortcuts work in both states, and ⌘K on the search and Space on Continue stay as control hints. Reversal: flip `showKeys` (`!guided`) at each call site; the provider's header comment states the rule.
- **The guidance migration is 0064, not the plan's 0063** (0063 had gone to the Product Researcher). Reversal: none needed; the plan's text refers to the number it expected.
- **`founder.preferences.get` keeps answering `{ date, time }`, and a separate `founder.preferences.guidance` carries the guided layer.** `DateFormatHost` seeds and optimistically rewrites the `get` cache entry from its cookie; widening the output broke its initialData type and its writes would erase the guidance fields. Reversal: merge the two reads once DateFormatHost writes through a selector.
- **`GuidanceHost` lives in `AppProviders` and decides "signed in" from the route** (`isFounderPath` minus `/`): the root providers have no server auth, and a read from a signed-out page answers UNAUTHORIZED and redirects to sign-in. Reversal: mount it per signed-in layout with the server's auth. **Reversed 2026-10-21 (review F28, perf audit item 2):** each signed-in layout (/b/[bid], LIVE, /new, /onboarding) reads the preferences on the server (`lib/guidance.server.ts`) and mounts its own `GuidanceHost` with them as `initial`, below `<Prefetched>`; the root mounts only `GuidanceRoot` (signed-out defaults, the definitions map loaded after hydration on signed-out pages). /businesses has no host and reads the defaults. The business glossary's words come from `BusinessShell` through `GuidanceTermsProvider`, not from the host watching the query cache.
- **`GuidanceHost`'s writes use their own mutation key** (`[["founder","preferences","guidance"]]`), so the two hosts' "another change pending" guards never count each other's saves. Reversal: one key for both if the hosts merge.
- **"The first profile save" means no live profile row before it.** An archived-and-recreated profile counts as first again; guidance is written off only when the experience is Exited or Operator, and never on an edit. Reversal: key the default on an account flag instead of the profile row.
- **Guidance reads as on outside a provider** (signed-out pages, stories, tests), so tutoring renders there and empty widgets collapse by default. Reversal: `DEFAULT_GUIDANCE_STATE.guidance = "off"`.
- **The "Add detail" section's open or closed state is remembered in the browser per account** (`foundmill.profile.detail.<accountId>`), not in `account_preferences`, whose `explainers_seen` is for explainers. Reversal: a key in `explainers_seen`.
- **Comfortable text is mirrored into an owner-scoped cookie `fm_text_size`, read by the root layout,** so the first byte carries `data-text="comfortable"` and nothing resizes after load; the cost is that the root layout reads cookies and the local session check on every request, so every route (`/login` and `/examples/*` included) renders dynamically. Reversal: stamp the attribute from GuidanceHost after the read and accept one resize. **Superseded 2026-10-21 (review F35, perf audit item 1):** the root layout reads no cookie; an inline head script stamps the attribute from the cookie before the first paint (`TEXT_SIZE_HEAD_SCRIPT`, `<html suppressHydrationWarning>`), so `/` and `/examples/*` are static again. GuidanceHost corrects the attribute and the cookie once the account answers, and sign-out clears the cookie. The script does not check the cookie's owner (it cannot know who is signed in); clearing it on sign-out covers a shared browser.
- **Comfortable text has its own rem base (`--fs-root` 13 px) apart from `--fs-body`**, so the mode changes type inside regions and never moves rem-based spacing, the 40 px header, 28/34 px rows or the 232 px sidebar. Reversal: fold `--fs-root` back into the html font size.

### The guidance primitives

- **`status-bad` and `status-info` were lifted to #F36066 and #5E8EFF** (the smallest lightness step to 4.5 : 1 on surfaces 3 and 4), with their glows; department and rating colors keep their values. Reversal: restore #F2545B / #5B8CFF and give pills on surface 3 a 16 % fill instead.
- **The Estimate / Forecast chip is a focusable button with a real Tooltip or the definition card**, like `Term`, instead of a native `title`. Reversal: a span with `title`, which a keyboard cannot reach.
- **`DefinitionCard` pins on Enter, Space or a click** and moves focus to "Learn more", because Radix sets every tabbable inside a hover card to tabindex −1; hover alone never pins it. Reversal: a Popover instead of a HoverCard.
- **Tooltips and definition cards open on focus only after a key that moves focus** (Tab, arrows, Home / End, Page keys; `keyboardFocus.ts`), never on a script's focus or after Enter, Space or Escape, so a dialog that auto-focuses an Estimate chip still closes on its first Escape. This replaced the wave A workaround of opening the Hire and Train dialogs on their first field (kept where it reads better). Reversal: `openOnFocus="always"` per card.
- **`Explainer` keeps a local dismissed state beside `explainers_seen`**, so "Got it" collapses it outside a provider too. Reversal: read only the provider.
- **The Form-key helpers are `formKeySentence` / `FormKey`** (and the Board's `costTrendKey`) so they never clash with the agents barrel's `formKey`, which now delegates to them. Reversal: a rename across the barrels.
- **`RatingBandPill` is an alias of `ScaleLabel`**, so the skill input and every scale share one band piece. Reversal: none needed.
- **A table's "…" row-actions button uses a native `title` ("Actions"), not the Tooltip component,** to avoid one Radix tooltip provider per rendered row. Reversal: the Tooltip if the design review wants the shared look.
- **A coach-mark tour is marked done only when it is finished or skipped;** a tour whose targets never appear never starts (it watches the page while waiting). Reversal: a time-out that marks it done.
- **Tour targets outside the shell are stamped at run time** (`useCoachmarkStamps` in `apps/web/components/tours/stamps.ts`) rather than giving `Coachmark` a CSS-selector mode, so `data-coachmark` stays the only contract; the stamps go once the widgets, the Inbox panes, the Explainer and the view selector carry the attribute (requested). Reversal: a selector field on `CoachmarkStep`.
- **When a tour ends, focus returns to the element that started it**, else to the last target only when it is a control (Continue after its callout), never to a region. Reversal: no focus move.
- **The tours may run on the example business;** they teach there too, so nothing special-cases it. Reversal: skip tours when `access.isExample`.
- **The first-Continue callout shows while `completedCount <= 1`,** not "until the first `cycle.completed`": commit starts cycle 1 by itself and the shell pauses coach marks while it processes, so the literal gate would never show it. Reversal: gate on `=== 0`.

### Words and scales

- **"Not stated" replaces "unknown" in the DecisionBar's hover card, and the inline line simply omits an unknown part.** `answered-panel` now names the guided fold's wrapper and the Panel is `decided-panel`. Reversal: rename the test ids back.
- **One /20 rating with a band word everywhere.** The Agents table first computed the mean of `skills[].displayed` on the client; `team.agents.list` now returns `rating20` and keeps `rating` (/10) deprecated for one release. Reversal: drop `rating20` and caption the table "/10".
- **Band words sit beside figures as `ScaleLabel`, compact in 28 px rows**, and `SkillGrid`'s `density="compact"` folds the word into the figure's tooltip where a row has no room (Training › Focus). Reversal: the pill everywhere and a wider column.
- **`StarRating` captions "Now 3.5 of 5 · could reach 4.5" wherever a potential is shown**, while its accessible name keeps "3.5 of 5, potential 4.5", which other tests assert. Reversal: one caption per screen only.
- **The Board's fit rings use the rating ramp's words, Excellent / Good / Average / Poor** (were Fair / Weak), so one number carries one word everywhere (§12 item 6). Reversal: the old band names in `FIT_RINGS`.
- **The Channels tab's Running view trades the Monthly budget column for Cost trend**, so the view still fits 980 px at 1280; the budget stays in All and Offline tracking. Reversal: swap the columns back and let the view scroll.
- **USD payroll figures read "AI cost (USD)" and wage caps "Wage cap (USD)"**; "wage cap" stays a defined term. Payroll's USD tile reads "AI usage cost (USD)" and falls back to "Team payroll" when the seed meters another currency. Reversal: the old labels.
- **Finance's department card labels runway "Months of cash left"**, while the status strip tile keeps the word "Runway" with the definition as its caption; the short-runway caption borrows the Inbox's "holds Continue". Reversal: "Runway" on both.
- **Palette Help rows are titled with the plain word ("Runway") and the one-line meaning,** not "What is runway?" (which reads badly for "Other roles it could take" or "Chief of Staff"); question words are stripped from the query so "what is runway" still matches. Reversal: question titles.
- **A blocked Continue opens the first holding note on the Inbox's Respond tab**, as the landing deck does. Reversal: open the note on All.
- **The top bar gained a first fit step, `chips-brief`**, which drops the chips' second phrases as soon as the search is below its full width, so they never cost the search its placeholder. Reversal: remove the step; the phrases then show only from about 1,550 px anyway.
- **A cash balance below zero reads "Cash is below zero" (Estimate)**, never "No limit" (`runway.ts` "negative" state, `runwayFigure`). Reversal: none sensible.
- **Help rows for a term with no `learn` target (Estimate, SKU, Vacation, Leaderboard) read in place and open nothing.** Reversal: open the DefinitionCard on select once the palette can host it.
- **Column-head Terms wait for DataTable's `meta.term`;** a Term inside the sort button would nest two buttons, so until then each word is defined one level up (the panel's help, footer, title or tile label of the same screen). Reversal: none; `meta.term` is the fix.
- **The Autopilot Term lives in the guided sentence beside the chip**, because a Term inside the chip's button would nest buttons. Reversal: an InfoTip beside the chip.
- **Field labels in Settings › Business stay plain:** a Term cannot sit inside a `<label>`, and Field's `help` tip would repeat the hint under the control. Reversal: `help` on the Field.
- **The InfoTip beside "Explain things as I go" renders with `always`**: it is information about the switch itself, needed most when guidance is off. Reversal: drop `always`.
- **Settings' key chip (Connected / None / Revoked) is derived from the business row** (model billing, the stop reason) the shell already holds, not from `keys.status`; on a plan that requires a key, a resumed business with a revoked key reads Connected until a new key is stored. Reversal: read `keys.status` with one more query.
- **"Let go" reads Not reversible**: there is no route that un-releases an agent. Reversal: an un-release route, then Reversible.
- **Team health counts an agent as "working" when it has a task in hand**, the department card's rule, not when its health is good. Reversal: the old count.
- **`TrainingResults.coachWords` rewrites the Coach's "House rules are untouched" to "Every rule still applies" on display** until the Coach prompt says it. Reversal: drop the rewrite when the prompt changes.
- **The Markdown term rule marks the note's source before parsing** (private-use sentinels around the first whole-word, case-insensitive match of each defined word, code, links and mentions skipped), so "first per note" is exact across re-renders; it wraps common words that are also terms (Form, Lead, Fit, Model, Policy, Session, Branch). Reversal: a per-note cap or an allowlist in `Markdown.tsx` if the second test finds it noisy.
- **A quick reply's second line renders inside the label column (`btn-desc`)**, under the words rather than under the key cap, instead of through Button's `description`. Reversal: Button's `description`.

### First run, Commit and the landing page

- **A variant's default name carries "(variant)",** and `naming.ts` strips both "(fork)" and "(variant)" from a business name. Reversal: the old "(fork)".
- **The profile's currency comes from residence, then the browser locale, then USD;** EU members without a listed currency (CZ, HU, PL, RO) map to EUR, and a saved profile keeps its currency. Reversal: ask for the currency first.
- **"I have no budget yet" is one radio card beside the amount;** typing an amount unpicks it and a saved profile with no budget reads as that choice. Reversal: a checkbox.
- **Target markets are no longer required on the profile:** an empty list saves the market derived from the residence (UK, US, EU, Nordics, DACH, Benelux, France, Canada, Australia, or the country's name), never from an unknown word. Reversal: require one market again.
- **The name checks are one open panel, "Check the name (domains and trademarks)",** not collapsed behind a toggle, so the domain and trademark rows stay visible and the specs hold. Reversal: a disclosure.
- **The credits line on Commit and the landing hero shows the free tier's grant ("$5.00") read from `packages/config`,** not the founder's actual tier; on Commit it hides when the paused-team notice applies. Reversal: read the founder's tier.
- **The Commit rail's chip summary was dropped** for the one "How it runs" line in the main column. Reversal: restore the rail chips.
- **"Your first day" is the landing's Step 04, the last beat,** as the plan words it, rather than third before "Press Continue". Reversal: reorder the steps.
- **The beat's Continue is the real `ContinueButton` rendered inert** with the screen-reader line "Then press Continue.", because the hero deck is where a visitor presses it. Reversal: a live button that scrolls to the deck.
- **"Your key, sealed" reads "Bring your own Anthropic key, or stay on the included credits…"** so it stays true now that credits exist. Reversal: none needed.
- **"Nothing is spent until you approve it" is kept verbatim** although the foundations drafting and the logo set draw a few cents of included credits before any sign-off. Reversal: "No building spend until you approve it".

### The Composer and agent prose

- **`because` sits on the recommended option only** in grounded decisions, the default options and the archetypes' Board options; the Board option schema refuses a recommended option without one. Reversal: optional everywhere.
- **The mock renders metric keys as plain words** (`METRIC_WORDS`: cpa → cost per customer, runway_months → runway (months of cash left at today's spending)). Reversal: none sensible.
- **The narrate test's AC-11.3.1 word floor moved from 40 to 20,** with the 180 ceiling asserted, after the prompt's 60-word floor became "no padding". Reversal: restore the floor with the prompt's.
- **The api's in-process composer sends the same two system blocks as the service** (the house rules, then the composer prompt v3), not its own one-line prompt. Reversal: none sensible.
- **A mock option handed over without a consequence states nothing** (the DecisionBar shows cost and reversibility) instead of "See note.". Reversal: none sensible.
- **Agent-facing rules and example plans keep the `request_human_action` action name;** only founder-facing prose dropped it. Reversal: rename the action, which touches the policy and the compiler.

### Home and the Inbox layout

- **The first-week checklist sits under the Inbox preview in its column** (x8, w4, h12 in the Founder's view preset), not strictly beside it, so hiding it leaves no hole; existing dashboards keep their stored layout and see it after Reset. Reversal: move the cell.
- **A hidden checklist renders nothing and keeps its cell** (one line in Edit mode), and completing it records the account-wide dismissal. Reversal: collapse the cell.

### The example business

- **The example is a viewer membership marked by a new `memberships.source` column** (`invite | founder | example`, 0065) with a check that an example row is a viewer; it is opened on the service connection (no RLS policy lets anyone insert their own membership), idempotent and audited, and leaving is a plain delete of the caller's own row. Reversal: drop the column; RLS never changed.
- **RLS is unchanged:** the 0048 read-receipt policy still lets an example viewer mark a note read for themselves (per reader, never shown to others); everything else a viewer cannot write. Reversal: a guard in `inbox.markRead` or a policy change.
- **`business.example.get` answers null without a committed demo business** (production without seeds shows no third door) and prefers Northwind, else the oldest demo; the door also hides for a caller who is already a real member. Reversal: a config flag naming the example.
- **`list`, `listNamed` and the seat count leave example rows out,** so an explorer is never staff, a seat, LIVE or one of the founder's own businesses. Reversal: count them.
- **Until KitBar has `continueSlot` and `businessBadge`,** the Example chip renders in the logo slot and Continue stays disabled as for any viewer (requested of the design system). Reversal: none; the slots are the fix.
- **The example founder's "GB / GB and IE" are written in the fields' own vocabulary** (residence "United Kingdom", markets "UK", "Ireland"), the example never touches the founder's name, and "Hide the example" restores only answers that still hold the example's value, so anything typed over it stays. Reversal: clear every field on hide.
- **Runway's example lives in the driver form and its own runway line only,** never in the chart or the warnings, so a lesson is never mistaken for this business's forecast. Reversal: drive the chart from the example too.

### Words in the definitions source

- **A definition's `learn` slug is written in the display vocabulary** (`team/agents`, `distribution/board`, `development/live-sessions`, `finance/payroll`) and resolved through `LEARN_SLUG_ALIASES` to the kept URL slugs, per CLAUDE.md "new identifiers follow the table". Reversal: write the URL slugs directly.
- **`PLAIN_LANGUAGE_TIERS.define` lists display words** (what a linter matches in text); `TERM_IDS` carries the ids. Generic words in it (Lead, Form, Health, Model, Session, Policy, Revenue, Competition, Contacts, Filing, Tested, Proven) rely on the linter's false-friend allowances. Reversal: a curated define list.
- **Sku, vacation, estimate and leaderboard have no `learn`** (no screen owns SKU; Vacation is a top-bar dialog; Estimate is defined on the chip; Leaderboard lives outside `/b/<id>`). Reversal: add targets when those screens exist.
- **A wave A test failure that looked like a regression was a rename,** not missing behavior: the api's "confirming a plan twice" failure was the renamed archetype task title ("Build the first prospect list"), fixed in wave B. Reversal: none needed.

### CI shard 1 (2026-10-22)

- **The seeded demo owner confirms 18+ even when the seed skips their profile** (`SEED_OWNER_PROFILE=0`). The attestation is the network's, not the profile's, and every network write asks for it. Reversal: confirm adults in shard 1 through the Network screen instead.
- **A table's own scroll body is a declared scroll region** (`data-scroll-region="table"` on `DataTable`). The design rule allows a table wider than its panel inside its own `overflow-x: auto` container; the no-clip check counts only panels and text that clip, not a table's scroll. Reversal: fit the CRM "All" view by dropping a column at 1280 px.

### Merges to main are the founder's (2026-10-22)

- **A delegated agent never decides `merge_to_main`.** Team › Responsibilities could name an agent to approve merges, and the routing then let it approve the merge at once. The founder asked to approve every pull request before anything merges, so the routing now treats `merge_to_main` like `sign_contracts`: the agent is left undecided and the founder answers in the Inbox. The routing test is updated to match. Reversal: drop `merge_to_main` from `founderOnly` in `packages/db/src/repos/approvals.ts`.
- **`run_checks` is held to the push settings** like the other GitHub writes (the repository chosen on Development › Code, pushing on). It starts the CI workflow on an `agent/` branch and needs no founder approval, because it spends no money and changes no branch.
- **The agents' checks run on GitHub Actions, not in a second sandbox** (2026-10-22). `run_checks` dispatches the existing `ci.yml` on an `agent/` branch and `get_checks` reads its check runs. The CI already runs the real checks (typecheck, lint, unit, Playwright, Storybook), so one set of checks covers the founder's PRs and the agents' branches. A separate sandbox would need its own Postgres, browser and seeds for the same result. Reversal: point the checks at a sandbox runner behind the same two tools. Commits keep `[skip ci]`, so the explicit dispatch is the only run on an agent branch.

### Import a plan (2026-10-22)

- **The import runs on the service connection, in one transaction, after the owner/admin and product-write check.** The vision lives on `business_foundations`, which app roles only read under RLS, so a single RLS transaction cannot write it; splitting the import into two transactions would let a failure leave half a plan. Like `team.agents.hire` and `agent.training.promote`, `product.plan.import` checks the role in middleware and every repo call is scoped by the caller's `businessId`. `product.plan.preview` reads under RLS. Reversal: a write policy for the founder on `business_foundations.vision`, then run the import inside `ctx.rls`.
- **No outbox event.** `product.spec.editBlock` and `product.roadmap.upsert` emit none, so the import reuses their writes and emits none either; the screen refetches and other tabs pick the change up through the routers' query invalidation. One audit entry (`product.plan.import`) records what was written.
- **A rewritten section reads as the file says.** The file's text goes into the section's first paragraph (the outline's target) as the founder's pinned edit; the section's other live paragraphs are archived (restorable from a published version), and the preview counts them. Sections the file does not name are never touched.
- **The founder's vision ends the first drafting.** Importing a vision sets the foundations row to ready (or creates the row for a business without one), so a pending or failed first drafting never writes over it; nothing is imported while a drafting runs. Acceptance criteria are appended to the item's description as an `Acceptance:` list (roadmap items have no criteria field), and `replaceUnstarted` only acts when the file has a roadmap.
- **The parser runs in linear time, outside any transaction.** A security review found that a 256 KB file could stall the api for minutes (backtracking heading and acceptance patterns, an unanchored trailing-space strip, emphasis pairs re-marking widening spans). The heading and acceptance checks are now single-pass, trailing spaces use `trimEnd`, emphasis spans are summed once through a difference array, a line longer than 10,000 characters (`LIMITS.planLineMaxChars`) is refused before any line is read, and the router parses before it opens the transaction, so a bad file never holds a database connection. Hostile-input tests pin each case. Not done: row locks on the "nobody has started this" read, so a task that starts during the import itself can still see its item retired; the window is one request long, and the item is archived, never deleted.

### Anthropomorphic agents (2026-10-22)

- **PORTRAITS: the image model is `gpt-image-2.5-flare`, not `gpt-image-1`.** OpenAI's deprecations page (read 2026-10-22) shuts `gpt-image-1` down on Oct 23, 2026 and names `gpt-image-2.5-sunburst` and `gpt-image-2.5-flare` as the replacements; Flare is the fast, everyday one and is plenty for a 1024 px headshot. The id lives only in `packages/models/src/routing.ts` (`IMAGE_MODELS.portrait_image`), not in `ROUTES`, because it is not a Claude route and is never priced in the usage ledger. Quality `medium`, size `1024x1024`, `output_format: "webp"`, `output_compression: 80`, `n: 1`. OpenAI bills these models by image tokens and publishes no per-image price, so the connector's `costMinor` (1 / 4 / 17 cents for low / medium / high) is a labeled Estimate.
- **PORTRAITS: `openai_images` is a platform-key connector (`auth: api_key, platformKey`) with one tool, `generate_image`.** `OPENAI_API_KEY` is read only by the gateway through `platformSecretsFromEnv` (the same path as `VOYAGE_API_KEY`). Its policy hints are `spendsMoney: true` (metered platform spend, like embeddings and sandbox compute; never the business's money) and `contactsThirdParty`, `requiresKitMode`, `irreversible` all false, declared explicitly. Agents get no grant to it (no capability maps to it); only the platform asks. The health check reads the model record (`GET /models/{id}`), which costs nothing, rather than rendering an image. Error text from the vendor is stripped of anything key-shaped before it reaches a log or a result.
- **PORTRAITS: the mock draws a PNG, so the tool's `mime` is `image/webp | image/png`.** The `persona_portraits` table accepts png, jpeg and webp, not SVG, so the placeholder is a small valid PNG drawn pixel by pixel (a smiling silhouette on a color taken from the prompt hash, 192 px, deterministic; `model: "mock-portrait"`). A real WebP encoder is not worth a dependency for a placeholder. The mock face is obviously not a photograph, which is the honest mock.
- **PORTRAITS: the gateway endpoint is `POST /v1/personas/portrait` and takes a catalog key, nothing else the image depends on.** Body `{ personaKey, businessId? }`; unknown fields (a `prompt`, a `size`) are dropped by the schema, an unknown key is a 400 `unknown_persona`, and the gateway builds the prompt with `personaPortraitPrompt` and fixes size and quality itself, so a stolen api token still cannot make the vendor draw anything but the 240 catalog faces. Service JWT `svc: api` only (403 otherwise). The optional `businessId` exists only because the audit log is per business: when given and the business exists, one `gateway.persona_portrait.generated` row records which business needed the face (an audit failure never loses a paid-for image). The rate limit is the platform's own window on the connector (`rl:platform:openai_images`: 20 a minute, 400 a day), not a business's, because a face is a platform asset. The response is `{ mime, base64, model }`; live mode needs `openai_images` in `LIVE_CONNECTORS` (or `INTEGRATIONS_MODE=live`), a missing key is a 503 `portraits_unconfigured`.
- **PORTRAITS: in mock mode the api draws the placeholder itself, without the gateway** (the same choice as `idea.domainCheck`): `connectorMode(env, "openai_images")` decides, so the golden path and the Playwright runs need no gateway; live, `ApiGatewayClient.generatePortrait(personaKey, businessId?)` asks it (120 s timeout, because a render takes 10 to 60 s). The gateway also serves the mock when it is the one in mock mode and the api is live, so the two disagreeing never calls the vendor.
- **PORTRAITS: `ensurePortraits` is in-process and best effort, by design.** A module-level set dedupes keys in flight (queued keys count), a queue of 3 workers bounds concurrency, and a failed key is left alone for 60 s (`PORTRAIT_RETRY_MS`) so a broken key or an unreachable gateway is retried by a later poll, never in a loop. Two api replicas can both make the same face once; `put` is an idempotent upsert and each worker re-checks `readyKeys` before it calls the gateway, so the cost of the race is at most one extra image per key, and the total stays bounded by 240 keys. A process restart forgets the queue; the next `team.personas.ensure` poll starts it again.
- **PORTRAITS: `team.personas.ensure` is a query on `businessProcedure` (any member, a viewer included).** The faces appear in the whole shell, not in the Team section, so a Team-section wall must not hide a colleague's face. It is a query although it starts work, because the web polls it until `pending` is empty (react-query `refetchInterval`) and repeating it is harmless. The input is only `{ businessId }`: the keys come from that business's own agents under RLS, so a caller cannot ask for, or learn about, another business's faces. It returns `{ ready, pending }` straight from `ensurePortraits` (one `readyKeys` read).
- **PORTRAITS: `GET /personas/:key` is public, cached for a day.** Faces are platform assets, so no sign-in; only catalog keys are looked up (anything else, and a face not generated yet, is a 404 with `Cache-Control: no-store` so a 404 is never cached before the image exists). A stored face is served with `public, max-age=86400`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: cross-origin` and a sandboxing CSP. The lead changed it from a year-long `immutable` cache: browsers would have kept the mock placeholder for a year after a deployment went live. Moving a deployment from mock to live faces: `delete from persona_portraits where model = 'mock-portrait'`, and the next `team.personas.ensure` regenerates them; browsers pick them up within a day.
- **DATA: `persona_portraits` has a uuid `id` primary key and `persona_key text not null unique`, not `persona_key` as the primary key.** BUILD §7 makes `id uuid primary key` the convention for every table and the migrations suite asserts `id`, `created_at`, `updated_at` and the `updated_at` trigger on every public table; the natural key is still unique and the repo works only by key, so nothing that uses it sees the difference. Content is capped at 8 MiB and the mime at png, jpeg or webp (SVG is never a face). Like `credentials` (0011): RLS on, no policies, `revoke all` from public, anon and authenticated, grants to `service_role` only.
- **DATA: persona keys are not unique in SQL.** The index on `agents (business_id, persona_key)` is a plain partial index: `pickPersonaKeys` repeats keys only past the catalog's 240, and a unique index would make the 241st hire of a business fail. Uniqueness comes from `assignPersonaKeys`, which takes a per-business advisory lock (`hashtext('agents_persona')`, the business id) inside the hire's transaction, so concurrent hires read each other's keys; `createMany` now runs in its own transaction (a savepoint inside commit's) for that reason. The column has a check (`^[a-z]{1,40}$`) and the repo validates against the catalog.
- **DATA: `taken` includes archived agents; `avoid` counts only live agents of the owner's other businesses.** A released agent's face never comes back as somebody else in the same business; a business the founder no longer uses still frees its names only when its agents are released. "Owner" is a `memberships` row with role `owner` (several owners each count; example memberships are viewers and never do), which is how `memberships.owner` and the owner lock model it; `businesses.created_by` is not used because a creator may have left.
- **DATA: the boot backfill runs in the background after `listen`, only when migrations succeeded,** one business at a time, each in its own transaction under the same persona lock, updating only rows whose key is still null. Every replica may run it at every boot: idempotent, and a failing business is logged and retried at the next boot. An agent without a key simply shows as today until then.
- **DATA: the demo seeds pass an explicit key from the business's own seeded order and ignore the owner's other businesses,** so Northwind and Ledgerly get the same faces whichever is seeded first, and a reseed of one never shifts the other. Live hires do avoid the owner's other businesses.
- **DATA: the Launch step's choice is written after the commit transaction, best effort.** It is the account's display preference, not part of the business, so a failed write is logged (`commitIdea` takes an optional `{ log }`) and the commit still succeeds; a second Take charge on the same idea writes nothing. Leaving the field out leaves the preference as it was.
- **DATA: beyond the brief's list, `crm.entity.lookup` rows and the desk's `ConversationPersona` carry `personaKey`.** The lookup reads it with `repos.agents.personaKeysByIds`, scoped to the business, so a record that points at another business's agent gets null (tested). `agent.profile.get`'s `lineManager` carries it too. The mirror type in `packages/dugout/src/components/conversation/types.ts` is UI's to extend.
- **UI: one display layer in the design system, not per screen.** `AgentPersonaProvider` lives in packages/dugout (EntityLink, Avatar and the conversation desk need it, and the design system may not import from apps/web); apps/web only supplies the preference and the face URLs.
