The saved-files-on-top model can overwrite newer work in a file both sides changed; the note names those files and the agent reconciles them, as for a hand-back.
- **Tasks that are the platform's own steps leave the loop at the next cycle (2026-11-02).** Dropping such tasks from new plans (#47) left the ones already planned, which dispatched every cycle and ended blocked within a minute each, with real tasks waiting behind them on dependency edges. The cycle now stops and archives them before drafting its plan, removes the edges from them (the platform does that step for every task, so nothing waits on it), and audits each one with the reason. Considered marking them done (a Plan that shows work nobody did) and leaving them for the founder to stop by hand (eight of them on the Foundmill business alone).
- **A held merge is a line in the Inbox, and GitHub's rules catching up is a retry (2026-11-02).** The merge review runs as an abandoned workflow so it outlives the cycle and the session; the price was that its final word reached nobody: the founder approved a merge, GitHub refused it seventeen seconds after the last required check passed ("required status checks are expected"), the review held it, and the Inbox said nothing. Two rules: a refusal GitHub takes back a moment later (not mergeable, status checks expected or pending, base branch modified) is retried by the workflow, and a merge that stays held is one Inbox warning in the founder's words, saying they already said yes and that the team tries again next cycle, never a blocker to Continue. Considered reading the workflow's result from its parent (the parent is gone by then by design) and polling GitHub's `mergeable_state` until `clean` before every merge (the state reads `blocked` for the whole wait and does not say why; the refusal's own words do).
- **A decision is two sentences, and a release never holds Continue (2026-11-03).** The Nov 1 three-question note (what happened, what is needed, what yes and no do, why asked) was right about the questions and wrong about the length: the founder, non-technical, read the first real round as far too much and asked for updates "much, much more simplified unless I specifically ask for more technical info". The note now says what happened and asks the one question; the options carry what yes and no do, the reading pane carries why the founder is asked, and Details carries the call. A release's decision no longer holds Continue: the merge waits in the background for the answer, nothing in the next round depends on it, and "Holds Continue" on it only made the founder feel chased. Considered keeping the labels and shortening the sentences (the labels were the length) and a per-founder switch for the fuller note (the fuller note has no reader yet; the Details disclosure already serves a developer).
- **The Inbox Composer and the Chief of Staff never narrate the engineering steps (2026-11-03).** A cycle update told the founder there was "no pull request and no approving review" and that the Chief of Staff "won't send a merge request until both exist", minutes after the platform had put two merge questions in the same Inbox; it also counted twelve tries and reported zero signups for a product still being built. Both prompts now say the platform runs the tests, opens and reviews the change, brings it up to date and asks the founder itself; a note is at most three short sentences, names at most two stuck things with what the founder can do, never counts attempts, and mentions customers only in a note about finding them. Considered a post-filter that strips sentences naming pull requests or reviews from the Composer's output (it would leave half-sentences and hide the defect instead of fixing the prompt) and writing cycle updates from a fixed template without a model (kept as the next step if the prompt alone does not hold).
- **A task nobody has the tool for stops after one attempt and becomes the founder's (2026-11-03).** The retry rule (Oct 28) is for work that can succeed next time; a task whose agent reports that no tool in the product can do it ("no tool is available to create or list business skills") fails identically on every Continue, costs a few cents a round and adds a blocked line the founder cannot act on. The cycle now stops and archives such a task after its first attempt, frees what waited on it, and tells the founder once, in one line and their words, that it is theirs to do or to drop. The agent's own block reason is the signal (`lacksTool`), never a hand-back. Considered letting the retry limit run out (the founder's Continue overrides it by design), asking the Planner to re-scope (the capability is missing from the product, not from the plan), and building the missing tools (adding sources and skills are founder actions by design: BUILD §2.2).
- **A code task is done when its work is on the default branch (2026-10-31).** Until now a code session's own report decided between done and review, and an agent that never said "review" ended done with its work on its branch: dependents started against a default branch without it. The task's branch decides now: a passing session on `agent/<taskId>` leaves the task in review, and only the merge (or GitHub showing nothing left to merge) makes it done. Tasks marked done the old way are found by the cycle's sweep and checked against GitHub before anything changes: merged or empty branches stay done, the rest go back to review and get their merge decision. A dependent that already started against the missing work is not stopped; it fails or blocks on its own, and the next cycle plans it once the dependency is merged. `merge_pr` marking a draft ready is part of the merge the policy already decided, not a separate permission.
- **The cycle sweeps review for merges, read from the record (2026-10-30).** The merge step starts from a finishing session, which left out every task already in review when it shipped and any review whose start was lost, and nothing plans a task in review again. A sweep at each cycle's start covers both from what is already recorded (the task's latest `task.completed`, its merge audit and its session's finish reason), so no new column or event is needed. It never re-asks a merge the founder rejected or let expire, and never merges a session held for a test-setup change; a merge GitHub refused is tried again each cycle, which asks nothing of the founder until it gets as far as an approval.
- **An answer after the session ended also reaches the approval's own wait (2026-10-30).** The api signaled `approval:<id>` only for approvals without a session (plan actions, channel launches), and the session for the rest; a merge review's approval has the finished session's id, so the founder's Approve would have reached nothing and the merge waited for the approval wait's hourly read. Now any answer given while no session waits is also sent to `approval:<id>`; when nothing waits there the signal is a miss the client reports as not queued, and the answer is stored either way.
- **Documentation only means nothing to test (2026-10-28).** A code task whose changes are all documentation files (by extension), in no package, and that names no test command skips the acceptance tests with a note and can end done; the fallback of every package's tests, one at a time, would spend minutes of sandbox time on a Markdown edit and prove nothing about it. Any non-documentation file (a root `package.json`, a workflow, a script) keeps the tests, and a task that names a command keeps it. The honest-outcome rule (decision 3) is untouched: the change is still saved, pushed and compared with the checkout.
- **Workflow tests stop from the world, not from a racing signal (2026-10-28).** In the time-skipping test environment, a workflow that only waits runs through its rounds and continue-as-new runs at full speed; a test that polls for a state and then signals the "current" run races that loop and hung for 120 s on a loaded CI runner (main, Oct 27). A test of continue-as-new makes the mocked activity return the stopping decision at the look it wants to be the last, and reads the run ids after the result: deterministic, no signal, no polling.
- **A checkout waits for an archive slot instead of giving up (2026-10-27).** The gateway's three concurrent archives (M1) protect its memory, not the sessions, and a cycle starts more sessions than that in one second: the orchestrator retries a `rate_limited` archive answer, pacing by the gateway's retry-after (5 s floor, 15 s cap), for at most twelve retries (about a minute at the floor: long enough for a burst of sixteen sessions to drain through three slots at a few seconds each, short enough that a session never waits a whole phase). The reason reported is the gateway's own, never "GitHub's rate limit", which it was not.
- **pnpm's safety settings travel in the environment, not in pnpm's global config.** pnpm 12 refuses `manage-package-manager-versions` and `package-manager-strict` in its global config.yaml (`ERR_PNPM_CONFIG_SET_UNSUPPORTED_YAML_CONFIG_KEY`), and under `set -e` that one refusal ended every acceptance run six seconds in. `export npm_config_manage_package_manager_versions=false npm_config_package_manager_strict=false` (`PNPM_SAFE_ENV`) at the top of both scripts reaches every pnpm command, the task's own included, and the `--config.*` flags on install and rebuild stay as a second pin.

### An approval goes back when the call never ran (2026-11-06)
The gateway marks a granted approval used before it calls the vendor, so two calls can never run under one approval. When the vendor then refuses outright (an HTTP 4xx other than 408, or the connector rejects the arguments or the tool), or the gateway stops before the vendor is called, the call did nothing and the approval is given back to the same session for the next try. Any answer that may have run (a 5xx, a timeout, an error with no status) keeps it spent: re-asking the founder is cheaper than doing an approved action twice. Accepted risk: a handler that makes several vendor calls and fails with a 4xx on a later one may repeat its harmless earlier steps (GitHub's merge marks a draft ready, then merges); each repeat runs under the same approved arguments.

### The Chief of Staff assigns skills, from the library or the repository's default branch (2026-11-06)
The founder asked that skills be assigned by the Chief of Staff, never by hand. Only the Chief of Staff holds the grant: a skill changes what another agent reads in its prompt, so one lead owns it and every change is audited. A skill file comes only from the business's own code repository at its default branch, read through the gateway pipeline and accepted only as a first-party read. A file reaches the default branch only through the founder's merge, so a file an agent writes on its branch, or text from anywhere else, cannot become a skill. Files get the same screening as a founder's upload. A skill grants no tools; it is instructions only. The founder can still add or remove skills on the profile, with the same limits.

### Every agent reads the team's documents, never the code, from the default branch (2026-11-06)
The founder approved read access for every agent. It covers documents only (Markdown and text files) on the code repository's default branch, through the same first-party reader as the skill tools. Code stays with the engineers' GitHub grants, so a researcher's context holds the handover, not the source. A document reaches the default branch only through the founder's merge, and every read is audited as the agent. Each answer carries a notice that the text is information, never instructions.

### Only a hard stop holds Continue; open decisions wait in Needs you (2026-11-07)
Founder (phase 1 of the first-time founder report): "When a user clicks continue, all rounds of work should run automatically." A decision no longer stops the team: the work that needs its answer is held on its own (an approval holds its task, a plan waits for its confirmation, the Chief of Staff plans only groundwork before the sign-offs). Only a warning the team cannot work past holds Continue: credits used up, a revoked key (0071, `holdsContinue`). The Respond tab is labeled Needs you, and a customer win is good news that never holds anything or changes the main button. The next change makes one press keep the rounds going automatically.

### Agents keep the founder's naming choice (2026-11-07)
Founder: whether agents go by people's names (Hung, Mirae) or plain roles "should still be a choice for the user as it is right now." Phase 2's "one name everywhere" follows that setting; it never forces one style.

### Notes that only inform close themselves (2026-11-07)
An agent's newer update archives its older open ones, and every Continue archives updates, reports and question-less warnings about finished, stopped or failed tasks, or untouched for 7 days. Archived notes stay readable in the Archive tab. Decisions, hard stops, wins and milestones are never closed automatically.

### A missing tool is the platform's gap, not the founder's chore (2026-11-07)
A task no agent has a tool for is stopped and recorded for the platform (`cycle.tool_missing_stopped`, a `platformGap` log warning). It no longer reaches the founder as "Only you can do this". Supersedes the Nov 3, 2026 one-line note.

### "Quality checks" is the founder's word for tests; "PR" is public relations (2026-11-07)
Every founder-facing status says "quality checks" (approvals: "It passed every quality check"). The word map's substitutes contain no engineering words themselves. "PR" left the map because a public-relations channel is a marketing word in the founder's vocabulary; "pull request" stays.

### No placeholder answers: runway, revenue and team health say what they wait for (2026-11-07)
Phase 1 of the first-time founder report ("one number per fact"). Cash-flow positive reads "Not running out" with "You earn more than you spend" ("No limit" read like a setting). Without a bank the figure reads "Not known yet" with "Not enough data yet: connect your bank". A quiet account, where nothing moved in the trailing months, is too little history, never good news: a stale or unused bank no longer reads as cash-flow positive. Revenue this month is 0 once a bank or revenue history exists, and "Not known yet" before; Team health with no agents reads "No team yet". Never a dash. Supersedes the 2026-10 "No limit" wording in "One runway vocabulary".

### Finance opens on three numbers; "Estimate" once per screen (2026-11-07)
Finance › Overview shows cash, net burn and runway first; revenue, MRR, ARR, margin, acquisition cost and payback sit behind "More numbers". The tiles no longer carry an Estimate chip each: one sentence under them names the figures that are worked out ("Estimate: … are worked out, not measured."). Every non-measured number is still labeled (CLAUDE.md), once, in words; the provenance tooltip on each tile still gives its source. The runway warning no longer says Continue is blocked (since 0071 only credits and keys hold it), and the warning legend says so. Home's "Customers this week" and the Revenue and customers widget count the same last 7 days. Follow-ups, not in this change: one server field each for revenue this month and customer counts (Inbox and the Chief of Staff still count their own windows), milestone MRR on the Finance definition, and the example business's data rolling forward with the calendar (its seed stays dated to the day it ran, which is why its Home goes quiet while Milestones and Finance still show the seeded history).

### Continue runs until done: one press, a run of rounds on the autopilot workflow (2026-11-07)
Founder decision (Nov 7, 2026): "When a user clicks continue, all rounds of work should run automatically." One press of Continue starts a **run** on the existing per-business `autopilot:<businessId>` workflow (signal-with-start of `continueRun`), not a second loop: the same activities, cycle ids and guard rails Autopilot uses, so Autopilot's schedule and a run never start two cycles at once. The run's rounds stop when `decideRun` (`packages/shared/src/autopilot.ts`) says so: the last round did nothing (failed, empty, or only waiting on the founder: decisions in Needs you never hold a run, 0071), a hard stop, a vacation, a stopped round, an archived business, Pause, or the day's limits. The press's own cycle keeps trigger `continue` (the founder's retry-everything rule); follow-on rounds use trigger `autopilot` so failed tasks keep their doubling waits and a failing task cannot loop. No 15-minute floor inside a run, only a 1-minute gap between starts. Choices taken here:
- **One set of limits, counted by trigger.** The daily cycle cap now counts every cycle started today with trigger `autopilot` or `continue` (`CAPPED_TRIGGERS` in `packages/db/src/autopilot.ts`), not only Autopilot's: a press and its rounds use the same cap and spend ceiling as Settings › Mode. Counting both triggers (rather than only cycles linked to a run) is simpler and also covers presses without a run (local mode, a failed signal); a vacation's cycles (`holiday`) and wake-ups do not count. The press itself always starts its first round, even at the cap (as Continue always did); the cap ends the run after it, and the end note says "press Continue for one more". So the cap bounds what starts on its own, never a founder's press.
- **No migration.** The run is derived from two events, `cycle.run_started` (written by the API with the `business.continue` audit row, at its timestamp) and `cycle.run_ended` (once per run by idempotency key `cycle.run_ended:<runId>`, written by the workflow's `autopilotRunEnded`, or by the API for Pause, Stop all agents and a lost workflow). Ends are matched by run id, so a run the workflow ended before the API recorded its start is still ended. A run with no end 36 hours on is not open (like a stale cycle). No table, so no new RLS policy or cross-tenant test; the business row stays untouched (Continue is open to members with write access, the row's policy only to owners and admins).
- **Pause ends the run**; the next press resumes a paused cycle and starts a new run that picks up after it (`cycleNumber: null`: the workflow waits for the open cycle, then decides). A press while a cycle runs (a second person, Autopilot's own cycle) also starts a run unless one is open. Signing off the roadmap starts the build through the same `startCycle`, so it starts a run too.
- **Processing for the whole run.** `cycleStatus().run` keeps Continue on processing with Pause between rounds; a paused cycle still shows Continue (it resumes).
- **Local mode is unchanged.** Without Temporal a press runs one in-process step and starts no run; the idle tooltip's "until nothing is left to do" describes the product with an orchestrator, which is every deployed environment.
- **Versioning.** The run handlers and paths sit behind `patched("autopilot-continue-run")`; histories recorded before (`fixtures/legacy-autopilot-*.json`, at daf80a21) replay unchanged. Delete them once no Autopilot started on the old code can still run.
- **Not changed.** Within-round parallelism, tier limits (free 4, pro 16, scale 64), `ORCHESTRATOR_MAX_SESSIONS` and the Automated-mode and vacation loops. In Automated mode a run and the `automated:` loop serialize through the shared cycle ids and the running-cycle wait (redundant but safe).
- **Known limits.** Agents that create tasks that create tasks are bounded by the daily cap, credits and the spend ceiling, not by the idle rule. A press racing an Autopilot cycle that is still planning (before `cycle.started`) may wait out the existing 60-second collision wait.

### The business's goal: the Chief of Staff proposes, the founder approves or changes it in words (2026-11-07)
Founder decisions (Nov 7, 2026, final):
1. **Who proposes, who decides.** Whoever the business's Chief of Staff is (`repos.agents.chiefOfStaff`, by its stored name, never a fixed one) proposes; the founder approves in one tap or asks for a change in words, and a change yields a revised proposal. The change is read by the `goalProposal` route (Sonnet, low effort) for the kind, the target and the date only; packages/shared writes every word the founder reads, so dates are US and no jargon gets in. The deterministic reader `parseGoalWords` is the fallback when the model fails or answers outside the rules (a past date, a wrong-kind target), and the mock route, so mock mode is exact. Words neither reads replace nothing and the founder is asked for a number or a date.
2. **The word is "goal"** ("Your goal"), the founder's own; the Planner's field stays "Objective" (the October decision), and only "goal moment" is banned.
3. **When the first goal is proposed.** After both sign-offs are approved and at least one `cycle.completed` later than the roadmap's approval (a business from before sign-offs needs only the round of work). The first proposal is a fixed rule with no model call: the first 10 paying customers in six weeks, or the next round number above 1.5 times the paying customers the business has (never below 10), written as the Chief of Staff's note. It runs on the API (`goal.ensure`, called by Home once per visit; Today calls it in part 2b), like `product.research.ensure`, so it needs no orchestrator and shows in mock mode.
4. **Kinds:** `paying_customers` (the Milestones counter: first payments), `signups` (signup conversions), `revenue_month` (this calendar month's operating inflow, the Finance figure, in the business's currency), and `custom` (no progress bar: "No number to track yet."). Every projection in the pace line is labeled Forecast.
5. **A missed date** proposes the same aim with a new date six weeks out right away (a Needs you note, never holding Continue, 0071), and the old goal stays active until the founder approves the new one. Judgment call: the same target rather than a new default, since missing a date says more about the date than the number. **Reaching a goal** marks it achieved (ingest, in the payment's delivery, and `goal.ensure`), posts a milestone-style note "Goal reached: 10 paying customers", and the next `goal.ensure` proposes the next goal (the next round number above 1.5 times where it stands).
6. **Any member with write access** (owner, admin, member with Inbox write; never a viewer or an example viewer) approves or asks for a change, like the sign-offs. `goal.ensure` writes nothing for anyone else.
7. **The vacation planner never answers a goal note** for the founder, nor does Automated mode (`GOAL_IS_YOURS`, "setting the goal is yours", beside the sign-off check; the vacation report lists it with that reason).
8. **Goal notes never set `blocks_continue`** (the repo forces it off whatever the caller writes).
Judgment calls taken here: one row per proposal (`revision` per business) rather than one row per goal with a history column, so a change request keeps the founder's words on the proposal they changed (`change_request`) and the revised proposal points at it (`replaces_goal_id`); at most one proposed and one active goal by partial unique indexes, transitions conditional on the status and serialized by an advisory lock. The archive exports `goals` under the product section. An open goal note cannot be archived from the Inbox (nothing else would propose while it is open). The demo seeds give Northwind and Ledgerly an approved goal from ten days before the seed date, so their Inboxes stay stable; a demo database older than the goal's six weeks gets a "new date" proposal on its next Home visit, which is the product working. The active goal is not yet in the Chief of Staff's cycle brief (a follow-up).

### Today replaces Home as the front door; Details keeps the dashboards (2026-11-07)
Founder decisions (Nov 7, 2026):
1. **Today is the business's front door.** `/b/<id>`, sign-in, `/start`, the last-business cookie, Take charge, the doors, Your businesses, the business switch (top bar and palette) and the not-found "Go to Today" links open `/b/<id>/today`. Today shows, in this order, Your goal, Needs you, This week and next, and a health strip. A new route rather than a rewrite of `/home`, so the dashboards, their saved layouts, shared links and the Home tour keep working unchanged.
2. **Details keeps the dashboards.** `/home` stays, titled "Details" (browser title "Details · <business> · Foundmill"), with its dashboard tabs; Today's tab bar is Today | Details. The sidebar's first item reads "Today" and keeps its id `home` and `fixed: "home"`, so G H ("Go to Today") and the Home tour's sidebar target follow it; `/today` and `/home` both light it. The palette lists Today (G H) and Details (keywords home, dashboard). The switcher's current-business action reads "Today".
3. **Spend is never summed across currencies.** The health strip shows "AI usage this week" in US dollars (the usage ledger's USD rows) and "Channel spend this week" in the business's own currency (channel metrics of the last 7 days; a channel kept in another currency is named in the caption and not added) when there was any channel spend; a business in US dollars with every channel in dollars gets one "Spent this week". All are Estimate. The ribbon's `spend_minor`, which adds AI usage in dollars to channel spend in the business's currency (0041), is not used for it. So the strip has four or five figures.
4. **The health strip's customer figure is Customers this week** (as Details), while the goal's bar counts all paying customers.
Judgment calls taken here: one tap on Today answers only where the Inbox needs no plan compiler (an agent's request with Approve when the reader can write the section it touches, a specification or roadmap sign-off with Approve, the goal with Approve this goal); everything else, Ready to start selling included, opens in the Inbox. `today.get` returns each part only to a reader with the section's read access (Inbox, Development, Finance), and one tap only to a writing member with Inbox write. "This week and next" is built from data with no model call (`packages/shared/src/today-words.ts`): finished work in the last 7 days by founder titles and the roadmap item most of it was for, what is in hand or next, blocked work as "waiting on something", the nearest Now or Next roadmap date (Estimate), and the round of work or Autopilot's next start (Estimate). Today mounts the blocker, the foundations and research ensure and `goal.ensure` (`components/home/lead.tsx`), as Details does; the Day one panel, the next step, Autopilot's chip and the first-week checklist stay on Details. A Today tour is a follow-up.


### Settings › What the team can do: one page that says truthfully what the settings do (2026-11-07)
Founder decisions (Nov 7, 2026, final; PR 1 of two, no migration and no change to what any policy enforces):
1. **"The Chief of Staff answers routine questions for you" stays bundled** in the third answer of "How much should the team do before checking with you?" (the Automated mode and its loop). A separate row needs `businesses.automated_authority` (0073), left for PR 2 if approved.
2. **Adding agents and changing an agent's model is a statement, not a choice**: "The team suggests it in the Inbox and you decide. When the team works and decides on its own, or while you're on vacation and you included it, the Chief of Staff can decide it for you." Checked in code: `hire_agent` and `set_agent_model` are internal plan actions that never ask (apps/api `services/plans.ts` `ACTION_TOOLS`, apps/orchestrator `activities/plan.ts`); they reach the founder as Inbox decisions, which only the Automated loop (`automatedMode.ts`, authority = every decision type but signing) and the vacation (its own authority list) answer.
3. **The spend line keeps three answers**: an amount, Always ask (0), and "Each agent's own limit (£250 unless you set one)" (null), the default read from `LIMITS.approvalThresholdMinorDefault` and shown in the business's currency, so the null answer never reads as "no limit". The chip reads "Asks before spending over each agent's limit" for it.
4. **The slug stays `mode`**; the tab is "What the team can do". Personal or live moved to Settings › Business (the Personal notice, Leaderboards and the Ask-to-connect blocker link there now). Team › Responsibilities stays editable with a pointer line; Playbooks stay where they are and the page links to them.
5. **Two wrong copies fixed**: the Assisted words no longer promise to ask "before contacting new people" (Cedar does not check whether a recipient is new), and "Empty means no cap" is gone (empty meant each agent's own threshold, default 250).
6. **The top bar's chip says the setting in plain words** with the business's currency symbol (`teamCanDoWords`, packages/shared `team-can-do.ts`), its tooltip the stakes and "Change it in Settings › What the team can do."
Judgment calls taken here:
- **The line is an upper bound, never a promise to spend.** Cedar's ask line is the smaller of the agent's own limit and the business's line (`effectiveApprovalThresholdMinor`), and templates hire agents at the 250 default, so a business line of 1,000 does not let a default agent spend 1,000 alone. The plan's chip wording "Decides alone up to £1,000" would have been untrue, so the Automated chip reads "Decides alone, asks over £1,000" (short "Asks over £1,000"), the question reads "Above what amount should a purchase always ask you first?" rather than "Spend without asking, up to how much", and an amount above the default shows "An agent still asks above its own limit when that is lower: £250 unless you set one on its Autonomy tab." The Commit default for Automated (four times the default) is unchanged here (PR 2 looks at Commit).
- **The spend answers stay editable under "Ask me before anything outside the business"**, with the hint that every purchase asks anyway and the amount applies on vacation (which runs as the third answer) and if the first answer changes. The plan disabled them; disabling would have hidden a value that is in force on vacation.
- **Each answer's promises are data** (`TEAM_CAN_DO_OPTIONS[].promises`: outside contact, permanent step, spend within and over the line, contract) and `packages/policy/src/__tests__/team-can-do.test.ts` evaluates them against Cedar through `agentPrincipal` for every answer × line (null, 0, 50, 250, 1,000) and on vacation, so the words cannot drift from enforcement. Augmented makes no contract promise: a contract draft that is internal runs in Augmented.
- **Delegating publishing to the Chief of Staff covers campaign launches, budget included** (the orchestrator routes a launch as `publish_content`); the row says "Includes launching an ad campaign with its budget." A request the Chief of Staff makes itself still comes to the founder (`routeApproval` never lets an agent approve its own request). Both rows write every department (`team.responsibilities.set` without a department) and go read-only with a link when departments disagree or someone else answers; `team.responsibilities.get` now returns `chiefOfStaffId`.
- **"Agents with their own setting"** counts an agent whose `autonomy.mode` moves it off the business's answer (as `agentAutonomy` reads lower/higher) or whose own limit differs from the default.
- **The chip drops the mode's name** ("Assisted · asks before spending" becomes "Asks before spending over £50"); the icon still tells the three apart, `data-mode` keeps the key, and Manage businesses keeps the mode word. The short form is its own span (`kitbar-chip-short`, shown from `chips-brief` until `chips-icon`).
- **Copy that named "Settings › Mode"** (the Autopilot notes, the run-ended toasts, Commit's reassurance, the terms) now names "Settings › What the team can do"; the Autopilot hint no longer says "hard gate" or "policy check".


### One name everywhere: an agent reads the same on every surface (2026-11-08)
Founder decisions (Nov 8, 2026, final; PR 1 of two, PR 2 "Leads first" is separate):
1. **The names-or-roles choice stays the founder's existing account preference** (Anthropomorphic agents, Settings › Preferences). Every surface follows it: the Inbox list, reading pane, sender line, Needs you on Today, toasts, live-session notes, Home and Details department cards, the Plan, pull requests, environments, code, live sessions, specialists, the release / duplicate / lend / model dialogs and the reassign and responsibility pickers.
2. **Background helpers always read by their role name**, even with Anthropomorphic agents on: the five utility templates (entity linker, classifier, categorizer, inbound sorter, moderation; `BACKGROUND_HELPER_AGENT_TYPES`, kept equal to `UTILITY_TEMPLATE_KEYS`). The Planner is not one and stays as it is.
3. **The rule: the server never writes a display choice into stored text.** Members of one business can have different preferences, and a stored note outlives a change of preference. Bodies name agents as `[[ent:<id>|<stored name>]]` (`agentMention`), which EntityLink renders under each reader's preference: the approval note (`packages/db/src/approvals.ts`), the rejection note (`apps/api/src/services/approvals.ts`) and the wage-cap notes (`apps/orchestrator/src/activities/dispatch.ts`). Subjects, option consequences, toasts and live-session notes stay plain text with the stored name and are named when shown, by `withAgentNames(text, roster, prefs)`. One path covers history and new notes alike; there is no backfill.
Judgment calls taken here:
- **One display function, `agentDisplay(agent, prefs, "name" | "label")`,** in `packages/shared/src/personas.ts`; `agentDisplayName` and `agentLabel` are aliases and `createAgentPersonaApi` uses it, adding `withNames(text, roster)`. `AgentIdentity` gains optional `layer` and `agentType` so the helper rule works wherever the caller has either; `department.list`, the roster rows, `today.get`'s sender and `inbox.get`'s approval agent now carry `layer` (and role and persona key where they lacked them).
- **`withAgentNames` replaces whole words only, case-sensitive, longest name first, never inside `[[ent:…]]` markup**, and gives a possessive the first name alone ("Malik's change is ready to go live", never "Malik (Head of Engineering)'s"). Text written under an agent's old name after a rename is left as it is.
- **The rejection note rephrases to avoid the possessive** ("You rejected the request from [[ent:…|Name]] to run …"), since markup is rendered as the label.
- **Verified and left plain:** the merge "is not live yet" subject (stored name; the merged change archives it by that exact subject) and session summaries (they go to the pull request body on GitHub and to the Composer as input, never straight to a founder).
- **Composer v6:** "Name an agent only as [[ent:<id>|Name]] markup with its id and its exact name from the manifest, never by a first name, a nickname or part of its name"; the subject uses the exact manifest name. The composer eval gains a deterministic third gate (`agentNamingHits`: renamed markup, a shortened name, an agent named outside markup in the body or an option line); the mock passes it on all 100 notes.
- **Sort, filter and CSV follow what is shown:** the Plan, pull request, environment and code rows' `agentName` is the persona label (`labelFor`) and the Agent cell passes it to EntityLink as `display`, so a table without a resolver still matches its field.
- **The department card finds its lead by id** (`lead.id`), never by name: two agents can share a stored name. `toSquad` keeps `personaKey` and `layer`; the lead carries id, role and persona key.

### Leads first: the Chief of Staff and the leads, their specialists a click away, background helpers out of the counts (2026-11-08)
Founder decisions (Nov 8, 2026, final; PR 2 of two, after "One name everywhere"):
1. **The Planner is not a background helper.** It takes coding and research tasks, so it stays a specialist under the Engineering Lead and keeps taking work. Background helpers are exactly the five utility templates (layer `utility`, `UTILITY_TEMPLATE_KEYS` / `BACKGROUND_HELPER_AGENT_TYPES`).
2. **Background helpers always read by their role name and leave the founder's lists and counts**: Agents tab rows and caption, Health groups, tiles and summary, Hierarchy nodes and summary, department cards and their team strip, the status strip's Team health, the palette's Ask rows, the reassign, responsibility, line manager and department lead pickers, Recruitment's catalog (the "Utilities" view is gone) and its Team moves table, and Agent of the week. They stay reachable through one honest line ("5 background helpers also run, linking names, sorting what comes in and checking drafts. They never take tasks or write to you. Show them") that opens a read-only list. A down helper is in none of the founder's health figures.
3. **No migration.** The tier is worked out from `layer`, `agent_type`, `line_manager_id` and `departments.lead_agent_id` (`packages/shared/src/team-tiers.ts`); `team.agents.list` and `department.list` add `tier`.
Judgment calls taken here:
- **The caption names the Chief of Staff instead of counting it as a lead**: "Chief of Staff · 6 leads · 23 specialists · 2 need you" (Northwind). The plan's "7 leads" counted it; naming it keeps the parts adding up to the rows on screen and keeps "lead" meaning a department's lead. "need you" is failing, down or degraded (the team-health definition) and only shows when any do.
- **Tier order: Chief of Staff, then background, then lead.** A helper set as a department's lead stays a helper; a non-designated second assistant ranks as a lead. A `*_lead` template is a lead even without a department.
- **The Agents tab groups specialists by the first group head their reporting line reaches** (a specialist managed by a specialist sits under that one's lead); a specialist with no lead in its department sits under the Chief of Staff, so the Chief of Staff's row can carry its own "+N specialists". A line manager that is a helper is not followed.
- **Search and filters open every parent with a matching child**; a row closed during a filter stays closed until the filter changes, and clearing the filter returns to the founder's own (collapsed) choice. Expansion is never saved in a view, so stored views keep working.
- **Sorting orders each level on its own** (TanStack sorts sub-rows) and the Chief of Staff stays pinned (`isPinnedRow`). The footer counts every matching row at every level ("2 rows of 9").
- **CSV is flat**: every matching row, open or not, each lead before its specialists, with a "Reports to" column (the Hierarchy's line, in the table's names; "You" for the Chief of Staff).
- **The leads come from the listed departments** (`department.list`'s and `team.agents.list`'s own department read, hidden ones excluded): no extra query. A lead of only a hidden department reads as a specialist until the department is shown.
- **The model dialog's "all agents" count stays the whole roster**: `agent.model.set` with `applyScope: "all"` still changes the helpers too, and the count says what the change does.
- **`pickAssignee` skips the utility layer even when a planner names one or a task row holds one**; before, only the worker filter kept them out. `team.agents.catalogue` leaves the utility family out unless `family: "utility"` is asked for.
- **Today needed no change**: it lists no agent counts, and helpers never write to the founder, so Needs you never holds one. The Today and Inbox naming rosters keep every agent (helpers read by role anyway).

### A native mobile app, built by Newly as a second client of the same backend (2026-11-08)

The founder asked for a Foundmill mobile app, built with Newly (app.newly.app, Expo / React Native), "a much more visual, much more intuitive and mobile-friendly version" that signs into the same accounts and runs the same businesses. `docs/mobile/` is the prompt pack: `AGENTS.md`, four skills (backend contract, design system, voice, product), the ordered prompts, and `BACKEND_CHANGES.md`.

- **The desktop-only rule stays, scoped to `apps/web`.** CLAUDE.md's "Never build mobile layouts. Desktop only (>= 1280 px)" and `NeedsLargerWindow` keep applying to the web app in this repo. The mobile app is a separate native client in its own repository (`foundmill-mobile`, Newly's GitHub sync); nothing responsive is added here.
- **One backend, no second one.** The app talks to `apps/api` (tRPC + superjson, bearer), `apps/stream` (the two-subprotocol handshake) and Supabase Auth (email one-time code / magic link) only. Newly Backend is not used for business data, and Newly's Supabase integration is never connected to the `foundermanager` project (it deploys migrations and Edge Functions). The app holds no secrets; the anon key, API URL and stream URL ride in `app.json`.
- **The product's words, tokens and rules are the web's.** The skills carry `docs/DESIGN.md`'s tokens translated to React Native (type two points larger, controls 44 px, radii still under 16), the banned-terms and US-English tables, and the product rules of Nov 7 (Today is the front door, only a hard stop holds Continue, Finance opens on three numbers, no placeholder dashes). Dark only, as the web.
- **Scope: a companion, not a replacement.** Departments, members, integrations, keys, the danger zone, permanent deletion and incorporation submission stay on the web and are reached by links that say why. Mobile-only engagement (a "Your week" card, an answered-within-a-day line) computes from data the API already returns and is labeled; no points, badges or invented numbers.
- **What the backend adds** (`docs/mobile/BACKEND_CHANGES.md`): the `foundmill://auth/callback` redirect and `{{ .Token }}` in the magic-link template; `founder.devices.register` / `unregister` and a `founder_devices` table (person-scoped RLS); per-kind notification preferences; a push fan-out through a `onesignal` connector in the gateway (real + mock) consuming `ev:` / `u:` events, declared as a `push.sent` event; later, `.well-known` files for universal links. Missing fields are added to existing procedures, never to mobile-only ones.

### Device tokens follow the latest sign-in on a phone; the mobile Supabase settings live in `supabase/config.toml` (2026-11-08)

- **A push token names one phone, and the account that signed in on it last owns it.** `founder_devices.token` is unique across accounts; `founder.devices.register` upserts on it with the service role, so a second account signing in on the same phone (a handed-over device, a co-founder on one iPad) takes the token over and the previous owner stops getting that phone's pushes. The alternative, refusing the token as "already registered", would leave a phone pushing one account's notes to whoever holds it now. A token carries no data: `list` and `unregister` run as the user under RLS, and nothing of the previous owner is readable through it. The row is account-scoped (like `account_preferences`), not business-scoped; the fan-out picks recipients per business through memberships.
- **Notification kinds are stored as a partial map, merged over defaults on read.** `account_preferences.notifications` holds only what the person chose (`{ "customer": false }`); every kind is on until turned off, so a kind added later is on for everyone without a backfill. The seven kinds (`NOTIFICATION_KINDS`) are the push kinds the voice skill names; `founder.preferences.update` merges a partial record (`z.partialRecord`, Zod 4) with `||`, like the guided layer's seen marks.
- **The email OTP is 15 minutes, 6 digits, for every client.** Supabase has one expiry for the link and the code; 900 s is long enough to type a code and short enough for a link left in an inbox (DEPLOY.md's admin-generated links expire with it too, so they are sent straight away as before). The web keeps working unchanged: the template carries both `{{ .ConfirmationURL }}` and `{{ .Token }}`.
- **`supabase/config.toml` is the record; the founder pushes it.** The CLI's `config push` is the one path that leaves a diff in the repo; this session has no Supabase access, so the three settings are ⬜ in DEPLOY.md's status table until the founder runs it or sets them in the dashboard.
- **`x-foundmill-client` is logged, never trusted.** It tells mobile traffic apart in the logs and the rate-limit reports; it grants nothing, and only a short printable value is recorded (`clientOf`).

### Push notifications ride the outbox relay's hook, through a service-only `onesignal` connector; `push.sent` is the record, not a ledger row (2026-11-08)

- **The notifier lives in the api, on the relay's `onPublished` hook, not in a stream consumer group.** The api is the one process that relays the outbox, and the hook sees every event of every business once (at least once) after it is published, with no Redis consumer, no new process and no new deploy unit. The notifier never slows the relay: the hook queues and returns, the work drains sequentially, every failure is logged and dropped. The cost of a business with no phones is one `founder_devices` query per candidate event and no gateway call. If push volume ever needs more than one process, `packages/events` `consume()` with a group is the next step; nothing in the contract changes.
- **A push is a courtesy, never a step.** No workflow, cycle or decision waits on a notification; a gateway or provider failure drops it (one warning in the log) and the Inbox still has the note. That is why the notifier retries nothing and why `push.sent` is written only after a send.
- **`onesignal` is service-only, like `openai_images`.** Agents must never be able to push to a founder's phone; the gateway refuses the connector on `/v1/call` and `/mcp`, Settings › Integrations never lists it, and only the api's service token reaches `POST /v1/push/send`. The keys are the gateway's alone.
- **Walls and preferences are applied where the words are made.** A reader without Finance reads no amount, a reader without the CRM reads no customer name, "Hide amounts on the lock screen" drops amounts for that reader, a kind switched off drops the reader, and no access to the event's section drops them too; agents are named the way each reader chose (anthropomorphic names and style). Readers of identical words share one send, so a founder and a viewer with the same walls cost one provider call. The gateway's audit row and the `push.sent` event carry counts and ids, never titles or bodies, which can hold a customer's name or an amount.
- **No `usage_ledger` row per push.** `docs/mobile/BACKEND_CHANGES.md` first proposed a zero-cost ledger row for the audit; the ledger is for metered spend (tokens, sandboxes, tools, training) and a free notification in it would dilute every cost report. The `push.sent` event (walled to Settings) plus the gateway's `gateway.push.sent` audit row are the record.
- **Which wins are pushed.** `customer.converted` is pushed only while `fullMatchDay` is true: past the 100th win of a kind the Journey already rolls the rest into the daily round-up, and the phone follows the same rule. A signup, an activation and a first payment each push once, with their own words.
- **The Newly project must connect Foundmill's own OneSignal app**, not the Newly-managed one: the server sends through that app's REST API key, so the app id in the phone's SDK and the key on the gateway must belong to the same app.

## Nov 9, 2026: a cycle's pause is matched by workflow id, not by time

- **`cycleStatus` reads a pause as the open cycle's when the audit row names its workflow id.** The time test ("pause row after `cycle.started`") missed a pause pressed while the orchestrator was still planning, because `cycle.started` is written after `planCycle`; the API then said "running, not paused" while the workflow held on the pause signal, and neither Continue nor Resume could free it. Workflow ids are unique per cycle (`cycle:<business>:<n>`, never reused), so the id is the right key; the time test stays only for rows written before the id was recorded.
- **The mobile Continue pill confirms a pause.** A single tap on a working round opens "Pause the round?" with Pause and Keep going; a paused round resumes on one tap. A stray double tap paused Commutair's first round after the key fix, which is how the status bug surfaced.
- **A plan refused for a bad key names whose key it was.** `planFailure` asks the routed model client whose key the business's call ran on and words the failure for that reader: Settings › Keys for a business on its own key, the platform's account (with an operator alert) for one on credits. The same split the billing refusal already made; a credits business cannot act on "check the key".
### Builder view: the engine behind one account-wide switch, off by default (PR 1 of 2: plumbing and hide-only gates) (2026-11-08)
Founder decisions (Nov 8, 2026, final):
1. **Operator and Exited profiles get Builder view on at their first profile save**, the same save that turns guidance off for them (`founder.profile.update`); everyone else starts off. A later profile edit never touches it.
2. **Tab names**: Live sessions reads "Working now", Pull requests "What changed", Environments "Live product" ("Live" alone is already a business mode). In this PR those tabs only get the founder titles and lose their technical columns (branch, PR number, lines, commit, provider; tokens and ETA on the tiles); their founder content (What changed panels, the Live product card, Working now tiles) is PR 2.
3. **Recruitment stays visible** (Hire links there) with its ratings shown as band words.
4. **A long outage still says something**: with Builder view off, more than 30 seconds without the live connection shows a quiet "Updates may be late" chip; a short reconnect shows nothing.
Judgment calls taken here:
- **Migration 0073** (the next free number after 0072_goals; journal idx 61): one boolean column on `account_preferences`, default false. The existing `user_id = auth.uid()` policy and grants cover it; the cross-account test is in `rls/account-preferences.test.ts`.
- **Off outside a provider.** `useBuilderView()` reads false with no provider, so a page or a test that forgot the provider shows the founder view, never the engine. The test fakes and Storybook default to on so the existing assertions about the engine keep holding; every gated surface has an off test.
- **The founder title applies only while the stored title is the module default**, so a founder who renamed "Live sessions" keeps their name in both states; Builder view on always shows the stored title.
- **A gated tab still opens by URL** (a bookmark, a shared link) with the gate state rather than a 404 or a redirect, so the link explains itself; the agent profile's Logs tab uses the same gate.
- **Settings › Departments lists the gated tabs with a "Builder view: …" chip** on the department row (the page has no per-tab list to put the chip on).
- **Ratings off are the band word everywhere RatingBar or RatingCell draws one**, CRM relationship strength and concept attributes included (the plan's "ratings everywhere"); the landing page's lead card keeps its figures inside `<BuilderViewProvider on>`. The replay scrubber's test marker keeps its word ("Tests passed") and suite without counts; a skill's hover keeps the task count but not the tested / proven / potential figures.
- **A view saved while off keeps its Builder view columns** (appended), and a Builder view view deleted or saved over while off is put back in its place, so turning the switch off never loses a builder's views.
- **Left for PR 2**: live-feed wording ("Cycle N started"), the sign-off pill ("Round N"), the subjects services write, the Composer's founder-words patterns, and the founder content of What changed, Live product and Working now.

### Automated mode and vacation never answer an agent's approval (2026-11-07)
An approval's note (an agent's gated call the policy asked about: Approve / Reject, `APPROVAL_OPTIONS`, or any note an `approvals` row links to) is the founder's whatever authority was given, like the product's sign-offs and the goal (`APPROVAL_IS_YOURS` in `foundersOwnReason`, apps/orchestrator `activities/holiday.ts`). Judgment call: skip it rather than resolve it through apps/api's `inbox.decide` path when the amount is within the line. The policy already said `ask` for that call, and the cases where an approval may be decided without the founder are already handled when it is opened: `repos.approvals.create` routes it through Team › Responsibilities and an agent handler decides it at once, so a note only reaches the Inbox when the founder (or a staff member) is the one to answer. Answering it from the loop would also need the approval's resolve path (the gateway's single use, the session's wake-up) in the orchestrator, which the founder never asked for. Automated mode's loop therefore leaves every approval in Needs you (counted in `queuedGated`); the vacation report lists it as "an agent's call waits for your approval" and counts it once, as the approval.
### Builder view (PR 2 of 2: founder replacements) (2026-11-08)
Judgment calls taken here:
- **What changed is its own rendering, not the builder's table with columns hidden** (`packages/modules/src/development/founder-views.tsx`). "Waiting for your go-ahead" lists the open code changes with Change (the founder title), Agent, Status ("Being checked" for `review`, "Ready for your go-ahead" for `merging`) and "See it" (the preview, "Not ready yet" before one); "Gone live" lists the merged ones with Change, Agent and "Put live Nov 7". A title with no founder title yet reads `founderPhrase(title)`, so a technical title never reaches a founder while the Composer catches up. `dev.prs.list`'s merged rows now carry `founderTitle` and `agentId` from their task.
- **Live product reads the recorded deployments** through a new read-only `dev.deployments.list` (the business's `deploy.succeeded` events, newest first, with the task's founder title; no table, so no migration and no new RLS surface: it reads `events` under the caller's RLS like `dev.prs.list`). Without it the card would claim nothing is live after every reload. "Your live product" is the newest production deployment with an address ("Updated Nov 7"); before one, it says "No release recorded yet", never that the product is down. "Previews of changes" are the open changes' previews (merged and stopped ones are not previews). The builder's Environments table is unchanged and does not call the new procedure (a follow-up could seed it from the same read).
- **Working now tiles open on the plan** and offer Plan and Preview only; Terminal and Diff, tokens, the ETA, the replay scrubber, the Task graph toggle (also the `formation` sub-view by URL) and "Open in Claude Code" are the builder's. The phase pill reads in founder words ("Building", "Checking", "Being checked", "Putting live"); the panel title is "Working now"; the tile names the task by its founder title.
- **The live feed keeps the raw events and describes them at render**, so switching Builder view rewords the feed in place. Off: "The team started a round of work", "Round of work finished · 3 tasks done" (and the other outcomes in the same shape), "Gone live: <founderPhrase(title)>", an agent summary with an engine number rewritten by `founderPhrase`, and the seeded rows named by founder title. `describeEvent`'s `builder` defaults to on, like the test fakes.
- **The sign-off pill reads "Waiting for you"** with Builder view off; the Product banner's line drops "Round N:" the same way. Nothing else in those files changed (the Request changes flow is being edited elsewhere).
- **Service-written notes are always in founder words**, since one stored note is read by founders and builders alike: "The team's last round of work did not finish" (the api's reconcile and the orchestrator's `failCycle`), "The team could not start work" (`notePlanFailure`), and the narrated note's fallback subject by outcome ("The team finished a round of work", "…reached its time limit", "The team is waiting on you", "…stopped"). The narrated tally reads "This round of work: 4 tasks started, 4 finished, 0 blocked." (tasks, not sessions). The words live in `apps/orchestrator/src/cycle-contract.ts` (pure, so the workflow bundles it); the change in `businessCycle` is string arguments only (no activity added, removed or reordered), so it is replay-safe, and there is no businessCycle replay test to update. The api's reconcile note names why without "workflow".
- **The Composer's word gate catches the engine's numbers**: `cycle \d+`, `round \d+`, `\d+ (of \d+ )?tests? (passed|passing|failing|failed)`, `\d+ tokens`, `PR #\d+` and `ETA` are hits for `founderJargonHits` and `founderTitleProblems`, and `founderPhrase` rewrites them first ("a round of work", "the quality checks passed", "some AI usage", "a finished change", "expected finish"). "PR" alone (public relations) and a funding round without a number stay plain.
### The founder's idea survives to the specification: use it as written, edit any card, sample content says so, a correction is the next task (2026-11-08)
From the first-time founder report (Nov 8, 2026, "Maya's First Day on Foundmill"): a founder who typed "students, cafes, same-day shifts, Manchester" was offered ten cards about renters and fitness instructors, could not change any of them, and was asked to sign off a specification about boutique fitness instructors; Request changes with the right words changed nothing she could see; Compare's full verdict read "Mock verdict md about distribution"; "United" counted money in US dollars. Group 1 of the report's fixes, the only group no open PR touches (PR #65 Builder view was checked):
1. **"Use my idea as written"** (`idea.write`, `services/ideation.ts writeIdea`, the studio's `WriteDialog`): one card from the founder's words. The Ideation agent rates it through `buildFounderCardRequest` (one card call, one draft of the allowance, the request names the founder's words as `founder_words`); the saved card keeps the founder's name and words whatever the model answers, its first version is `action: "edited"` (the history reads "Written by you"), and its meta carries `founder_words`, `brief` and, in mock mode, `sample`. It goes straight to Commit; the deck keeps it.
2. **Edit on every card** (`idea.edit`, `EditDialog`, the `edit` concept action in dugout's More menu): the name, the pitch and the kind of business as a new version with the ratings carried over (`repos.ideas.addVersion` now takes `archetype`, written on the idea row). A changed pitch becomes `founder_words`; a rename alone does not. The card reads "Your words" while its pitch is still those words (`founderWrote`: a later merge or variant that rewrites the pitch drops the chip).
3. **The founder's words are trusted inputs of the foundations.** `FoundationsIdea` gains `founderWords` and `brief` (read from the committed version's meta by `repos.foundations.inputs`); `foundationsTrustedBlock` sends them in the trusted JSON (never inside an untrusted block: they are the founder's), `knownNumbers` counts their figures as facts, and both foundations prompts (versions 3 and 2) carry `FOUNDER_WORDS_RULES`: founder words are the ground truth for who, what and where; the card's title and summary give way to them; the business is called by its Commit name, never the studio's working name. Every drafted card now records the brief it was drafted from (`cardProvenance`), so even a founder who never edits a card has their brief reach the specification.
4. **The mock layer reads the brief instead of inventing an audience** (`briefFacts` in `packages/models/src/mock/inputs.ts`: the people before a struggle, what they struggle to do, the product sentence; a "for" or "helps" phrase counts as people only when it names people). Mock cards echo the brief's audiences and outcomes on most cards (every fourth roams, so a set stays a set), the first card leads with the founder's own product sentence, and the "built around <keyword>" padding goes when the brief named someone. The mock foundations build the vision, Problem ("In the founder's words: …"), Customers and Value proposition from `founder_words` and replace the card's working name with the business's. Compare's verdict is written from the cards' ratings (`generateCompareVerdict`: leaders, weakest, which to commit and why) instead of the schema synthesizer's placeholder. A typed question on a concept is answered as asked: quoted back, with the card's closest evidence named as such, "the card has no price in it yet" for money questions, and "Find out for me" offered.
5. **Sample content says so.** `Concept.sample` (meta `sample: true`, set by the api whenever the mock model wrote the card) renders a "Sample" chip with its tooltip on every ConceptCard, and Commit's note under the card says whether the pitch is the founder's words or the agent's draft (with "Edit it in your words first" back to the studio).
6. **Request changes is the next thing the team does** (`services/signoff.ts`): the revision task is titled "Revise the specification from your notes" (never the founder's sentence cut off), carries their words as its goal, has `REVISION_TASK_PRIORITY` (10, ahead of every planned task) and is started at once through `startTaskByHand` (moved from `routers/dev.ts` into `services/task-start.ts`; Plan's Start calls the same function). The follow-up note says who is on it and that nothing else is built until the new version is approved; when it could not start now (no orchestrator, the team stopped, the budget used up) it says so and that Continue starts it. `SignoffResult.revision` reports which. Building still starts only when both rounds are approved (`bothSignedOff`), so a round sent back holds the build by construction; the goal proposal after the first round is untouched.
7. **Small fixes from the same report**: the profile summary reads "£1,500", never "GBP 1,500" (`budgetWords`, used by `profileSummary` and the studio's `profileLineOf`); a country typed only part of the way ("United") asks the founder to finish picking (`partialCountry`: the start of one or more listed countries and not one itself) and leaves the section unticked, while a full country, a short form ("UK") or a name the list does not hold still pass.
Judgment calls:
- **Mock cards still vary.** A brief that names people could make every card the same idea; every fourth card draws an adjacent audience from the archetype and only the first card uses the founder's product sentence, so Compare still has something to compare. A brief that names nobody ("surprise me", "AI playtesters for indie games") draws from the archetype's audiences exactly as before, so the recorded fixtures stay stable.
- **A founder-written card is rated, not unrated.** An unrated card would show ten empty bars and the mock foundations would have no strengths or risks to name; one card call costs one draft of the allowance, the same as Import's single card, and the ratings are labeled as the agent's (the pitch alone is the founder's).
- **"Your words" follows the words, not the action.** A card the founder edited and then merged has a pitch the agent wrote again; the chip reads the meta against the current pitch, so it never claims words the founder did not write.
- **The revision starts through the same path as Plan's Start**, refusals included (a stopped team, a paused agent, no room on the plan tier): a sign-off never bypasses a limit a founder set. The refusal reaches the founder in the follow-up note instead of a thrown error, because the sign-off itself succeeded.
- **The staged drafting's untrusted blocks are unchanged**: the card's title and summary still travel as untrusted data; only the founder's own words moved to the trusted side.
- **The eval checks the words verbatim, not a paraphrase.** `FoundationsCase.mustStore` (the new Door A case `foundations.door_a_founder_words`) fails a draft whose stored text does not carry the founder's words whole; the grader's rubric then judges whether the rest builds on them. A paraphrase that keeps the meaning is exactly what the report complained of, so it is not good enough.

### Continue always answers: one sentence from the api, on screen within a second, and the orchestrator's absence said before the press (2026-11-08)
From the first-time founder report (Nov 8, 2026), P0 "Continue must always answer": on the documented laptop stack the founder pressed Continue, "No cycle running" stayed for a minute, a toast they never saw said "One new note" about a milestone, Start on a task answered "The orchestrator did not take the session, so the task did not start. Try again in a moment" forever, and the top bar read "Reconnecting…" to a stream service that was not running. Group 2 of the report's fixes:
1. **The api writes the sentence, not the shell.** `business.continue` returns `summary` (`services/continue-summary.ts`): exactly one of "Cycle N started: K tasks ready to build", "Cycle N started: nothing is ready to build yet" (with why: the sign-off waiting in the Inbox, the drafting, a plan with nothing left), "The team cannot run right now: the orchestrator is not reachable" (no agent worked; the one note that stood in is named and opens from the toast), "Cycle N resumed", "Cycle N is already running". The shell's `describeContinue` shows it as it is. The facts (dispatchable tasks, the product stage from the foundations row and the latest sign-offs; a selling business needs neither) are read in the same wave as the cycle reconcile, three statements the budget test now allows (20 → 23).
2. **Something changes within a second of the press, whatever the server does.** `AppShellProcessing.notice`: the strip's accessible sentence and the tab bar's line read "Starting the next cycle…" from the mutation's `onMutate`, then the api's headline for 15 s or until the orchestrator's `cycle.started` reaches the stream and the strip's own sentence takes over. The line shows for everyone while a notice stands (the guided-only processing line was never visible to the founder in the report), in the secondary ink and as a status, so a screen reader hears the answer too. The toast stays, with the detail.
3. **The orchestrator's absence is known before a press, from one place.** `TemporalService.health()` answers from the last connect verdict without waiting (null until the first attempt has answered; a due retry runs in the background), `business.get` carries `orchestrator.reachable`, and three surfaces read it: Continue's tooltip caveat ("a press writes one note; no agent works"), Development › Plan's banner "Tasks cannot start right now" with Start off in the column, the row menu and the panel, and the api's own sentence after a press. Unknown reads as fine everywhere: a page never opens with a warning it cannot back.
4. **"Live updates off"**, not "Reconnecting…": a business channel that has never been live since the page opened reads off after 30 s (`connectionNotice` `everLive`), Builder view or not. A channel that was live and dropped keeps saying what the socket does.
Judgment calls:
- **The in-process step stays.** Without an orchestrator the press still runs the one composer note (the PGlite stack's loop, the golden path and a dozen tests rely on it); what changed is that the founder is told, in the strip and the toast, that no agent worked and what to do. Removing the step would have made the documented local stack a dead button; dressing it up as a cycle is what the report rightly called dishonest.
- **The sentence names the number of tasks, not the plan.** "K tasks ready to build" is what `repos.tasks.dispatchable` returns at the press (planned or ready, nothing open before them), which is what the orchestrator will dispatch first; the Planner's own work (new tasks from the roadmap) is not promised, because the press cannot know it yet.
- **"Orchestrator", never "Temporal", in front of a founder.** The health reason keeps the service's own words for logs; every sentence a founder reads says "the orchestrator, the service that runs the team's work" and tells them whose job it is to start it.
- **The caveat shows only on idle.** While a cycle runs or a hard stop blocks, the button's own words are the news; the caveat would compete with them.
- **"Round of work" for everyone, "Cycle N" for Builder view.** The api's summary carries both lines (`headline`, `plainHeadline`); with Builder view off the strip and the toast say "The next round of work started: 6 tasks ready to build", the words the live feed and the team's notes use since PR #66, and "Starting the next round of work…" on the press. Agreed with the Builder view session on Nov 8, 2026.
- **The notice clears on `cycle.started`, not on `cycle.status` refetches**: a refetch may still read the queued cycle as "The team is working", which agrees with the notice; the orchestrator's own first mark is the moment the strip knows more than the press did.

### Approvals must be decidable: what exactly would run, always shown; no content and no GitHub are not yes/no questions; same-kind launches are one decision (2026-11-08)
From the first-time founder report (Nov 8, 2026), P0 "approvals must be decidable": "QA Engineer wants to run a change to the data layout on GitHub for 'Verify and review As a returning user…'. OK?" with Details empty, Approve "Recommended, Not reversible", no GitHub connected, and five "wants to launch campaign. OK?" notes once distribution started. The product's own rule ("every decision arrives with the work done, a recommendation and the reason for it") was exactly what these notes lacked. Group 3 of the report's fixes, under the rule agreed with the Builder view work (PR #65): hide how the platform runs, never what is being decided.
1. **"What exactly" is the founder's block, outside Builder view.** `approvalWhatExactly` (packages/db) turns the call's tool and arguments into rows per kind: code (files, branch, change, lines, a clipped diff; a migration reads as "A change to how the business's data is stored: <file>"), release, campaign (channel, campaign, goal, budget, each draft's copy; the api loads the campaign and its drafts the arguments only name), message (to, subject, message), spend (what, amount, period), channel, other (the remaining arguments as labeled rows, ids left out, long values clipped). A session's one-line `argsSummary` is the headline when nothing else is there, or a "What exactly" row under the kind's own sentence. The reading pane shows the block right after the sentence and before who asked; the call's name, the rule's sentence and the raw arguments stay Builder-only behind Details, as PR #65 left them.
2. **The tool's name alone is not content.** A migration with no file and no summary, a message with no recipient and no text, are `empty`: the note reads "… but did not say exactly what it would run. Ask for the details before deciding.", Reject is the recommended option under the label "Ask for the details", and a rejection the founder typed nothing on carries `ASK_FOR_DETAILS_NOTE` to the agent, so the next ask comes with the files, recipients, copy or amounts. Approve stays available as "Approve anyway": a founder who knows the agent may still say yes, with the note's own word for what that is.
3. **No GitHub, no "OK?".** A GitHub call for a business with no connected repository (none, archived or disconnected; a mock installation counts, it has somewhere to run) is worded as what it is: "… but no GitHub repository is connected to <business> yet, so it has nowhere to run. Connect one first.", with "Connect GitHub first" recommended and a link to Settings › Integrations in the pane. The approval row is still written (the agent asked; the hold is real), so a later Approve runs it once GitHub is there.
4. **Same-kind launches are one decision.** A `launch_campaign` approval that opens while another waits joins the waiting one's note instead of opening a second: the note is revised in place (`repos.inbox.revise`) to "N channels want to launch a campaign" with each channel's budget and the total, one Approve all / Reject all answers every approval linked to it (each recorded and delivered on its own: its wait, its event, its plan action), and the pane shows one expander per request with its own what-exactly. Team › approvals keeps answering one request at a time; a batched note closes only when none of its requests is still open.
Judgment calls:
- **Content, not shape.** The rows read the arguments by meaning (`files`, `to`, `budgetMinor`, …) and fall back to labeled rows for anything else, so a new tool is never a blank; what a reader's walls hide stays hidden here as it does for the arguments and the amount.
- **The requester is not blocked at the raise.** The report asked that a request without content "must not be raised". Refusing at the gateway or the orchestrator would stop the agent's session with no founder in the loop and break the mock recordings that carry only a summary; the note raised instead tells the founder it is not decidable and sends the agent back for the details on one click. The orchestrator's own `argsSummary` line is honored as content when it is the only thing there.
- **Only campaign launches batch, for now.** They are the one kind that arrives in a burst by construction (every channel engine runs the same week). Code changes and messages are each their own decision: batching them would hide the one the founder would have refused.
- **A batch never crosses a business or a settled note**: the join looks only at this business's open approvals whose note is still unread or read; a decided or archived note is never reopened.

### Show the work, cost and vocabulary: "No artifact yet" over "Merged", a priced Continue, a Commit that explains itself (2026-11-08)
From the first-time founder report (Nov 8, 2026), P1: Pull requests said "6 merged" with "—" in the PR column and Environments "6 merged" with no URL while Code said "No saved code yet"; Commit's "Personal · Assisted · Marketplace" chip row meant nothing; Product became Editorial unannounced; wages were USD caps with no total; a cycle had no price before the press.
1. **"No artifact yet" is the honest word when nothing exists outside the platform.** A done code task with no deployment and no repository connected is a `no_artifact` environment, a queue merge with no pull request, commit or repository carries a "No artifact yet" pill, and both Development screens open with "What exists now": where the code is (a repository, the simulated one, or nowhere), how many previews can be opened, and what to do about it. Unknown reachability (the repository query still loading) says nothing: never a warning the screen cannot back.
2. **Commit says what each choice decides.** Four labeled lines replace the chip row: Where it runs (Personal or Live, who sees it), Who decides (the business mode's own tagline and the spend cap), Kind of business (what the archetype decides), On a schedule (what Autopilot does). The `data-summary` values are unchanged, so the tests and the e2e read the same five facts.
3. **The roster hides background helpers and prices the team.** `idea.preview` marks the platform's utilities (`background`) and Commit leaves them off the list as the Team screen does, counting them in a trailing "plus N background helpers". The wage caps of the visible agents add up to one line, an Estimate, in the credits' currency.
4. **A renamed department is introduced, not renamed.** `idea.preview` carries each department's standard name; the chip reads "Editorial · your Product department" and one line says which kind of business calls it so and that it does the same work.
5. **Continue has a price.** `cycle.status` carries the last finished cycle's cost; the button's tooltip ends with "Last cycle cost $0.52 · Estimate".
Judgment calls:
- **Wages stay in US dollars.** The report asked for the business's currency. The platform holds no exchange rates (the ECB connector is a finance import, not a rate table the api can read at Commit), and a made-up conversion would be a figure nobody can trace; the line names the currency it counts in and the one the business counts in, so nobody adds them up.
- **Today keeps its words.** The report asked for "What exists now" on Today too. Today's week summary is the Chief of Staff's sentences from data; a second surface saying where the code is belongs to Development, where the founder goes looking for it, and Today already points there.
- **The last cycle, not a forecast of the next.** The next cycle's work is its own; the last one's cost is a measurement with an Estimate's caveat, which is what the founder needs before pressing.

### Polish from the first-time founder report (2026-11-09)
Judgment calls in the P2 items:
- **The domain list is stamped with when this browser received it.** The registry's `checkedAt` can be cached, and the mock's is a fixed time; "Checked 9:00 AM" read at 2:50 PM told the founder the list was stale when it was seconds old. The question the line answers is how fresh the list is, so the client's clock answers it; a call that failed stamps nothing.
- **The Inbox tour waits for the list.** A tour that starts over a note the founder opened competes with the note; one that unmounts when a note opens loses its place. So it starts the first time the Inbox is in front with no note selected and then stays, and its first card sits at the list's foot.
- **The example business is introduced, not replaced.** The report asked for a smaller or week-one example. Seeding one is a content project (a business, its team, its notes and its history), out of scope for a polish pass; until then the door says plainly what it opens: a business two years in, whose spend and customer figures are its own and not a forecast of the founder's.
- **Nothing to change for the returning founder.** Sign-in already lands in the last business through the last-business cookie (the magic-link callback and the start page both read it); the report's fresh account had no business, so onboarding was right.

### Founder areas: a sidebar built on the founder's questions (PR A: registry, design system, navigation) (2026-11-09)
Founder decisions (Nov 9, 2026, final):
1. **Company is a section inside Money**: the legal module's tabs go to Money (`AREA_SECTIONS.company`), and its Entity tab reads "Company" while Builder view is off. No Settings › Company route.
2. **G chords follow the areas**: G P Plan, G C Customers, G M Money, G R Product, G T Team; G H Today and G I Needs you stay. Departments lose their sidebar letters and Settings › Departments loses the letter picker; the `departments.shortcut` column and `department.setShortcut` stay (no migration).
3. **Builder view on shows every tab inline**, with no "More detail" menu.
Judgment calls taken here:
- **Plan always shows**, because Milestones is always one of its tabs (it was always in the sidebar before); Customers shows when the person may open a department over distribution or read the CRM; Money, Product and Team only through their departments. The plan said "a department in the area"; this keeps Milestones reachable for someone walled from Product.
- **An area's badge** sums the unread notes of the departments whose first module is the area's, so a merged department is counted once (in its first module's area).
