- **A department with no modules** is its own sidebar item after Team (dot, badge, letter), its letter chosen by `departmentLetters` with the shell's reserved letters (H, I, P, C, M, R, T, G) on top; a stored letter that is now an area's is ignored in the shell, not rewritten in the database. The api's stored-letter rules are unchanged.
- **A founder's own tab** (a slug no module has) is "default" tier and goes with the department's first module's area.
- **The promotion rule is per department**: a visible department with no inline tab shows its first visible tab inline (Formulation Lab › Specialists), so Northwind shows 15 area tabs and Ledgerly 14.
- **A hidden department opened by URL** keeps its own tab bar (the area does not list it), with the area still in the breadcrumb.
- **Development's Plan reads "Work list"** while Builder view is off, so it never reads like the Plan area; Builder view keeps the stored "Plan" (the Code screen's "Go to Plan" button is module copy and stays).
- **Breadcrumbs only on department routes** ("Product › Formulation Lab"); `/crm` and `/milestones` show their area's tab bar and keep their titles without a breadcrumb row.
- **The gate's "Open" button and "No such tab"** go to the department's first inline tab in its area (Development: What changed), not its first tab.
- **Copy names a screen by its area** (`moduleTabLabel`, the palette's Generate rows: "Customers › Board", "Team › Recruitment"); service-written notes that name a department tab ("Distribution › Board" in the start-distribution note) are left for PR B.
- **The sidebar's Needs you keeps the id `inbox`** and links to `inbox?tab=respond`; the screen keeps the title "Inbox" and the palette row "Inbox" (with "needs you" as a keyword).
- **Not in this PR**: the AreaLine opening sentences and `area-words.ts` (PR B), "Explain this page" (group L).

### Founder areas PR B: each area opens on one plain sentence (2026-11-09)
Judgment calls taken here:
- **Where the line lives**: the words are pure (`packages/shared/src/area-words.ts`); the design system's `AreaLine` only lays them out (sentence, chip, section lines, kind line, skeleton); `packages/modules/src/areas/AreaOpening.tsx` (`@foundmill/modules/areas`) reads the data through the widget data source with the **same paths and inputs** as the area's tabs, so the line and the tiles share cache entries and requests and never disagree. apps/web renders it on department routes in an area (`areaOpeningOf`), `/crm` and `/milestones`. No AppShell slot: AppShell is being edited elsewhere, and the line belongs to the page, not the chrome.
- **The label goes to the chip only when it closes the sentence** (`withProvenance`); a label in the middle stays in words ("on pace (Forecast)"), so Plan reads its pace as a Forecast and its next roadmap date as an Estimate without two chips. A past roadmap date ("the next was due Nov 2") carries no label: it is a date that passed, not a projection.
- **Money** reads the runway tile's months to one decimal ("about 1.4 months"); "more than comes in" is said when gross spending is above net (money comes in). Short history says the cash (measured, no label) and why the months are missing; a balance below zero keeps the Estimate label, as the runway tile does.
- **The Company line counts 90 days** (the plan's window), not the Compliance tab's 30-day "Due within" tile, and says the window in the words; overdue filings are said first. It counts compliance events and filings once each, as the calendar lists them, and calls them filings.
- **Customers** reads the channel window's blended totals (the same 35 days the Board and Attribution label); "most from" names the channel with the most customers, else the most leads. "Selling has not started yet." when `distributionStartedAt` is empty.
- **Product** counts changes that went live in the last 7 days (merged, `dev.prs.list`) and every open code change as waiting for the go-ahead (the What changed panel's own list); with nothing live in the window it adds when the live product last changed (production `deploy.succeeded`). Without Development access it says the team builds from the signed-off specification and roadmap.
- **Team** counts the founder-visible tiers (background helpers left out); "down or failing" (agent status `injured` or `failing`) stands in for the plan's "need you", which read like Inbox notes. The Chief of Staff is "<name>, your Chief of Staff" when the reader sees a name of its own (a persona or a renamed agent), else "Your Chief of Staff", so the role is never said twice; the name is an EntityLink with `personaText="name"`.
- **The kind line** shows whenever the department's name is not the area's label (Development, Finance, Legal and the founder's own names), not only for renamed departments: telling a renamed department from a default one would need a list of default names, and departments are labels over modules.
- **The Overview's own summary sentence stays** under the tiles for now (it says the same thing in the Finance tab's words); folding it into the opening line is left for "Explain this page" (group L).
- **Service copy**: the cycle summary's and the Chief of Staff's channel-wait reason, the commit note, the Board's engine hint and the research note now say "Customers › Board" and "Product › Work list". The sign-off notes in `apps/api/src/services/signoff.ts` (and the first-days spec that reads them) still say "Distribution › Board" and "Development › Plan": that file is being edited in another change.


## 2026-11-09 — One "Explain this page" button instead of a banner on every tab (Phase 3, part 3)

- **Why.** A first-time founder saw a surface-2 explainer strip on every tab, right under the area's opening sentence. The founder's report: one "Explain this page" button replaces the "What this screen is for" banners.
- **How.** `Explainer` keeps its props and export, so no call site changed. It registers its lines (screen id, purpose, action, watch for, example handler) with `PageExplainProvider` and renders nothing in place. `ExplainPageButton` (ghost, small, `BookOpen`) opens a popover (the design system's `Popover`) with the three labeled lines (`EXPLAINER_LABELS`), "Show me an example" when given, and Close. Escape closes it and returns focus to the button. No auto-opening.
- **Placement.** `AppShell` mounts the provider around its header and content and draws the button in the tab row's trailing slot, after the processing line, so it sits at the right end of the row and never adds to the 40 px header. A page with breadcrumbs and no tab row gets it in the breadcrumb row. Every screen with an Explainer (department tabs, the agent profile's Training tab) is inside the shell and has a tab row. The button keeps `data-coachmark="explainer"` and `data-explainer="<screenId>"`, so the department tour (which targets `[data-explainer]`) and the specs still find it.
- **What `explainers_seen` means now.** "The founder has opened this page's explanation", set on the first opening (it used to be the "Got it" click). The stored shape is unchanged. The unread dot shows until then; Settings › Preferences "Replay the tour" and "Show the first-week checklist again" clear it, so the dots come back. The `guidance.explainer_dismissed` event keeps its name and now fires on the first opening.
- **Last mounted wins.** When two Explainers are mounted on one page, the last one registered is shown, and unmounting it hands the page back to the one below. A more specific screen mounted later (a tab inside a profile) explains what the founder is looking at now.
- **Inline fallback.** With no provider above (stories, isolated tests, the full-screen Ideation Studio at `/new/ideate`, which has no shell header), the Explainer draws the same button and panel in place, so it stays usable without edits to that screen.
- **Removed.** The collapsed/open strip, "Got it" and the chevron. The `grid-cols-explainer` token is no longer used by the Explainer.

## 2026-11-09 — The Chief of Staff on every page answers what's happening, what's blocking and why (Phase 3, part 4)

- **Why.** The founder's report: the Chief of Staff on every page, answering "What's happening?", "What's blocking us?" and "Why am I seeing this?" in plain words, from the same facts as Today.
- **Deterministic answers from Today's facts, no model call.** `chiefOfStaff.answer` (`apps/api/src/routers/chief-of-staff.ts`, `services/chief-of-staff.ts`) gathers the facts with Today's own reads (`needsYouFor`, `weekFactsFor`, `goalView`, `cycleStatus`) and hands them to the pure `chiefAnswer` (`packages/shared/src/chief-answers.ts`), which reuses Today's sentence builders (`weekDoneSentence`, `weekNextSentence`, `weekRoundSentence`, `weekWaitingSentence`, the goal's pace line, `goalValueText`, `goalPaceState`). The last two moved from `apps/web/components/today/today-view.ts` to `packages/shared/src/goals.ts` (today-view re-exports them), so Today and the answers read the goal identically. `weekFactsFor` takes an optional already-read cycle status to avoid a second read. A model call would cost money on a "what's happening?" tap, could contradict the page next to it, and would need its own eval; a stale-by-seconds read of the same facts cannot. It is a query (`staleTime` 15 s), not a mutation: nothing is written or audited.
- **Walls.** Each part follows the reader's section access exactly as `today.get` does: Needs you needs Inbox read, the work needs Development read, and a revenue goal is masked for a reader walled off Finance (`goalView`). A reader without either gets the goal only, or "There is nothing to report yet: the team has not started, and no goal is set." The procedure is a `businessProcedure`, so a user of another business is refused.
- **Labels.** The pace sentence keeps "(Forecast)" and a roadmap date "(Estimate)" because the sentences come from the same builders as Today. Dates are US ("Mar 4, 2099" when more than 300 days away, else "Mar 4"). Agents and records are `[[ent:<uuid>|Name]]` via `agentMention`, rendered by `EntityText` as EntityLinks under the reader's naming preference; the answer's plain text also goes through `persona.withNames` (the roster Today uses), so a stored name in a note's subject reads as the reader's preferred name.
- **What each answer says.** *Happening*: a stopped team first (with the reason), else vacation ("You are on vacation until Nov 21; the team keeps working on its own." because a vacation runs rounds), then who is doing what now (or what is next and how work starts), what finished this week, and the goal's pace. *Blocking*: the team stopped (with why), Needs you (count, the first note and its sender, how many hold Continue), a round that did not finish, a paused round, and work waiting on other work with which work it waits for (from `task_edges`; there is no stored "blocked because" text, so the reason is the unfinished work ahead of it). Vacation is not a blocker. Nothing: "Nothing is blocking the team." *Why*: the page's purpose, then why it matters now (a count from Needs you, the goal for Today, Details, Plan and Milestones, agents working for Team, "Now/Next" for Product). At most four sentences each.
- **Page context.** The client sends `{ path, screenId, area, purpose }`. The registered Explainer's `purpose` is client-side text, so the host (`apps/web/components/shell/AskChief.tsx`) passes it when it is a plain string of at most 300 characters (the server strips any `[[…]]` from it and refuses a longer one); the area is derived from the path and the department's modules (`ask-chief.ts`); the server falls back to the area's words, then the page kind's (`chiefPageKind`: Today, Details, Needs you, area tab, agent profile, Settings, Contacts, Milestones, journey, other). Pages with no registered Explainer therefore still answer. The three starter labels live in `CHIEF_QUESTION_LABELS` and are also the first message of "Continue in chat".
- **Placement.** One control per page, drawn by the shell: `AppShell` takes a `pageActions` node and draws it in the tab row's trailing slot just before "Explain this page"; in the breadcrumb row (with the Explain button) when the page has breadcrumbs and no tabs; and in a thin 32 px row of its own (`page-actions-row`) when it has neither, so no page lacks it and a page never has two. Deviation from the brief: "Explain this page" is not drawn in that thin row, because a page with neither row never had one and adding it there is a separate change. Hidden when `chiefOfStaff.who` is null (no Chief of Staff) and until it loads (no placeholder; the row does not move because the trailing slot is right-aligned).
- **Name and portrait.** `chiefOfStaff.who` (the oldest live assistant, `repos.agents.chiefOfStaff`) returns the id, stored name, role, avatar seed, persona key, layer and department; the label is `persona.nameFor(...)`, so "Ask Ines" with Anthropomorphic agents on and the stored name otherwise. No name is hard-coded.
- **"Continue in chat"** opens the existing agent desk (`deskApi.open`) with the question as the founder's first message, exactly as the palette's "Ask <agent>" does; that message is a normal conversation turn, so the desk's reply (not the popover's answer) is where a model may be called. "Ask something else…" opens the same desk with nothing typed.
- **Not done / left.** No eval (no agent behavior or prompt changed). The answers are not streamed into the Today page or cached on the server; each open is one read of about the same cost as `today.get`.

## 2026-11-09 — A first-week timeline on Today; empty screens say what will fill them (Phase 3, part 5)

- **Why.** The founder's report, group K "A first week that explains itself": Today says what the team does on Day 1, Days 2–3 and in Week 1 and what the founder will be asked and when; an empty screen says what will fill it and when, never a bare dash or a skeleton that never ends.
- **Where the facts are read.** `today.get` gains `firstWeek` (`firstWeekFor`, `apps/api/src/services/today.ts`), one field instead of a new procedure, so Today stays one read that the stream already refetches. The words are the pure `firstWeekTimeline` in `packages/shared/src/first-week.ts` (tested without a database). `business.firstWeek.get` (the seven checklist flags on Details) is unchanged: the timeline needs days, not flags.
- **Visibility rule.** The panel shows while the business is under 10 days old (`FIRST_WEEK_DAYS`, counted in the business's time zone from the day it was created) and has not started distribution (`distributionStartedAt` empty); otherwise `firstWeek` is null. Reasoning: starting distribution is the last first-week milestone, so a business that is selling is past it, and a business older than 10 days that never got there is not helped by a calendar it has missed (Needs you and the goal panel carry it on). A business past the rule costs no extra read: it is answered from the business row. The seeded Northwind and Ledgerly are signed off and selling, so they never show it, with no sample-specific branch; an example viewer of a brand-new demo would see it, which is correct.
- **What drives each step (state is read, never stored).** Day 1 is done when both the specification and the roadmap are approved (`product_signoffs`; its date is the later `decided_at`). Days 2–3 is done when the first round of work has finished (the earliest `cycle.completed` event) and a goal has been approved (any `goals.approved_at`); its date is the later of the two. Week 1 is done when distribution started, which also hides the panel, so it is never shown done. "Now" is the first step not done, the rest are "next". The calendar never moves a step: a slow business stays on Day 1 on day 4, with its window labeled Estimate.
- **Judgement call: the goal sits in Days 2–3, not Day 1.** The Chief of Staff proposes the first goal after the first round of work (Phase 2b), so asking for it on Day 1 would promise a note that cannot exist yet. Day 1 asks for the two sign-offs; Days 2–3 asks for the goal and "the first changes going live"; Week 1 asks "Ready to start selling?" and any spend over the founder's limit. While part of a step is open, its sentence names only what is still open ("You are asked to sign off the roadmap.").
- **Dates.** Done steps carry the real day ("Done Oct 8"); the others a window labeled Estimate counted from the creation day (Day 1: that day, Days 2–3: the next two, Week 1: days 4 to 7: "Oct 10 – 13 (Estimate)"). US form, en dash ranges, a year only when far from today (`goalDateWords`). A stopped team (`agents_stopped_at`) keeps the steps and adds one line: "The team is stopped, so nothing moves on until you resume it."
- **Component.** `FirstWeekTimeline` (design system, `components/today/`, story `Today/FirstWeekTimeline`): an ordered list in a 12-column Panel, `aria-current="step"` on the step under way, read-only, no motion.
- **Empty screens: one table.** `emptyWhy(id, facts?)` in `packages/shared/src/empty-why.ts`, ids: `today_needs_you`, `leads`, `campaigns`, `contacts`, `milestones`, `roadmap`, `spec`, `spec_versions`, `cash_history`, `cash_flow`, `bank`, `entity`, `officers`, `compliance`, `hired_agents`. Each is one sentence of what will appear and what makes it appear ("Leads appear here once the team starts selling, after you answer “Ready to start selling?”"). Facts are optional (`firstDays`, `distributionStarted`); module screens do not read business facts, so they use the sentence that holds either way, and only Today passes `firstDays`. A "when" is never a calendar promise beyond what the timeline states ("usually on day 1").
- **Scope kept.** Whole-panel and whole-screen empties only: no real 0 and no table cell's "—" changed; skeletons stay while loading. Development › Pull requests and Environments are left alone (another change adds their "What exists now" panel; the table's "nothing … yet" phrasing is compatible). Not touched: Distribution Board, Campaigns' side panels, the agent profile's empty panels, Runway's footer.
- **Not done.** Only Today has a Playwright check of the timeline against a fresh business plus one area tab (Customers › Leads); the other changed empty states are covered by their module tests. No eval (no agent behavior changed).

### The sample business says it is a sample, its numbers agree, and it rolls forward with the calendar (2026-11-10)
Phase 3 of the first-time founder report, group K item 3 ("Home: 0 customers this week, no revenue. Milestones: 1,000th customer. Finance: £25K a month recurring and £76K over 3 months. Runway: 'No limit'").
1. **A quiet "Sample business" marker on every demo business.** A one-line caption under the business name in the top bar (KitBar `businessBadge`, `lib/access.ts sampleMarker`) reads "Sample business"; its line is the second line of the business switcher's own tooltip, under "Switch business" (KitBar `businessBadgeLine`): "Its figures are made up for the example: nothing here is real money or real customers." It shows wherever `businesses.is_demo` is set (only the seed sets it), whoever opens the business: the seed's own owner sees it too, since the figures are made up for them as well. It never shows on a founder's own business. For an example viewer it replaces the "Example" chip (one chip, not two) and its line adds "Nothing you do here is saved."; Start your own and the rest of the Example frame are unchanged. Your businesses' example row keeps its "Example" chip with the same line.
2. **Where each figure came from, and the fix.**
   - *Home "0 customers this week, no revenue"*: the seed dates its activity up to the day it ran, and a deployment seeded weeks earlier had nothing in the calendar's last 7 days or month. Fixed by rolling the sample forward (point 3). "Customers this week" is now one server field, `journey.ribbon.customers` (`thisWeek`, `previousWeek`, `total` = every first payment), counted live from conversions in the business's days whatever range is asked. Home's tile, Today's health tile (the same builder) and Revenue and customers read it; the rows stay the spark. Revenue this month already had one source (`finance.overview.revenue.monthly`, the shared `revenueThisMonthMinor`).
   - *Milestones "1,000th customer"*: correct (Northwind's seed has about 1,070 first payments), but nothing beside it said so while Home read 0. The cabinet panel now says "1,073 paying customers so far" from the same `customers.total`, and the seed test checks that every customer milestone is reached exactly when the first-payment total has passed it.
   - *Finance "£25K a month recurring" vs "£76K over 3 months"*: one fact (the 3-month revenue ÷ 3), but the tile called it monthly *recurring* revenue for a skincare brand whose customers buy once, and neither caption named the months. `finance.overview.revenue.recurring` (shared `revenueIsRecurring`: b2b_saas, consumer_app, services and content_media recur, as the synthetic journey models them) names the tile "Average monthly revenue" and ARR "Yearly revenue at this rate" when revenue does not recur (no MRR term then); a subscription business, or one before its archetype, keeps MRR. Both captions name the same months: "Aug – Oct 2026, 3 full months" and "a month: the Aug – Oct 2026 revenue ÷ 3" (in the person's date format).
   - *Runway "No limit"*: already gone (Nov 7: "Not running out" with "You earn more than you spend"); a stale seed instead read "Not known yet: needs three months of bank history" because the trailing months were empty. The roll-forward fixes that; the words are not re-decided.
3. **The sample rolls forward with the calendar, in place (`rollDemoForward`, packages/db `seed/roll-forward.ts`).** Considered: shifting at read time (every query of every screen would need the offset), re-seeding when stale (deletes example viewers' seats, communities' links and takes minutes during which there is no example). Chosen: at api boot (after SEED_ON_BOOT; the workflow reconciler waits for it at most 10 seconds) and every hour, each demo business (by its fixed id only) is moved forward by the whole days since its anchor, in one transaction: every timestamp and date column of the sample's own rows (the ids the seed recorded in `demo_sample_rows`) and of its `businesses` row, `created_at` and `updated_at` capped at now, unique dates moved in two steps (parked a century ahead, then back), the goal sentence's date rewritten, Northwind kept the newer business and the daily rows refreshed. The anchor is the seed's today (now in `seed.demo`'s details; an older seed's entry is read by its own time) or the last `seed.demo.rolled` audit entry, read under an advisory lock (shared with the seed's rebuild) so two api instances move the data once. Rows anyone writes after the seed never move, and tables that record real people, real money, secrets, consent or the network are never touched (see "The sample's roll-forward: only the sample's own rows" below). Migration 0074 lets the updated_at trigger keep the shifted value for the rows of the business the roll names in `fm.demo_roll_forward`; every other write is stamped now() as before. Known limits: text period keys (`ledger_snapshots.period`) and timestamps inside JSON (inbox payloads, milestone refs) keep their seeded values, so the seed's prose no longer names absolute dates ("1,000th customer reached.", "You are away for 11 more days."). `DEMO_ROLL_FORWARD=off` turns it off.
4. **The concept card's button reads "Choose this idea"** (`CHOOSE_IDEA_LABEL`, the card, Compare's "Choose <name>" and its ranking rows). The press picks the idea and opens its setup (name, logo, how it runs); Take charge there starts the business, so the card does not say "Start this business". The step keeps its name, Commit.
5. **The profile summary names the place and the markets** ("First-time founder · full time · £25,000 · based in the United Kingdom · markets: Nordics"), on the form and under the studio titles (`placeAndMarketsWords`). Cause of "Nordics" for a founder in the United Kingdom: the summary listed the saved target markets bare, so a hand-typed market read as where the founder is (the onboarding spec and `completeProfile` type "Nordics" for a UK resident on the shared stack, and the unit test expected "£25,000 · Nordics, EU" for one). The region map and the derived default were right ("United Kingdom" → UK).

### The sample's roll-forward: only the sample's own rows, never wedged, a scoped trigger, an off switch (2026-11-10)
Security review of PR #74 (the roll-forward above). Findings and what was decided:
1. **Which rows are the sample's: an explicit list, not a time.** The first version moved rows created at or before a cutoff and set the next cutoff to `min(old cutoff + days, now)`, so after one roll nearly every row a real person had written (an owner seat's approvals, real usage, credentials, community posts) counted as the sample's and moved from then on. No time-based rule can work, because the sample's own rows are moved into the days in which real rows are written. Chosen: the seed records the ids of every row it wrote, per table, in `demo_sample_rows` (0074, one row per business and table, `row_ids uuid[]`; business-scoped with standard tenancy, read-only for app roles, a cross-tenant spec, left out of the export), just before its `seed.demo` entry. A roll moves those rows and nothing else. A row on the list that someone changes after a roll (its updated_at, stamped now() by the trigger, is newer than the roll's `settledAt`) leaves the list at the next roll, so a seeded approval the founder resolves keeps the time the audit log records. Considered: a provenance column on every table (about ninety migrations' worth of churn), the max seeded created_at per table (overlaps real rows after one roll for the same reason). A seed older than the list gets one at its first roll from the rows created up to its own entry, which is exact while nothing has rolled. Judgement call: a table without updated_at cannot show that a person changed one of its seeded rows, so such a row keeps moving.
2. **Excluded tables.** Never touched: the audit chain, the outbox, memberships, read marks, saved table views and dashboard layouts (people's), leaderboard rows, posts and community members (the network's: demo posts sit in public communities among real replies), usage, credit balances and checkouts (billing periods), credentials and webhooks (secrets), the suppression list (consent), and `demo_sample_rows`.
3. **A unique key never wedges the roll.** In production the nightly agent rollup writes `agent_metrics (agent_id, day)` for the demo businesses and the first version shifted last night's row onto tonight's, failing the whole transaction every hour. With the list the rollup's rows never move; a sample row whose landing key another row holds (the rollup's day, a channel engine's day; null keys compared as equal, so a day with no campaign is never doubled) is dropped and the writer's row wins. The move back from the park uses the same ownership filter (a newer row with a far-future date is never moved). A unique index whose key is an expression over a moved date cannot be checked, so its table is left alone; any other unique or exclusion violation rolls back that table alone (a savepoint per table). Both are named in the result, the log and the audit entry (`skipped`); such a table then lags by that roll's days.
4. **The trigger exemption is scoped.** `fm.demo_roll_forward` now holds the id of the business being rolled; the trigger keeps the written updated_at only for that business's rows (`business_id`, or the `businesses` row's own id) and never for the authenticated or anon roles, so a signed-in session that sets the setting cannot pin updated_at. 0074 is edited in place (not on main yet).
5. **Boot, refresh, off switch.** The workflow reconciler for every business waits for the boot roll at most 10 seconds, then starts whatever the roll is doing. Northwind is put first inside `rollDemoForward` after any move. A failed refresh of the journey's daily rows is logged and retried on every pass until it succeeds (the move is committed by then). `DEMO_ROLL_FORWARD=off` (default on) turns the roll off.
6. **The Sample business chip only on a demo business.** `sampleMarker` requires `isDemo === true` in both branches, and the shell's context passes the business to `accessFor`, so an example-source membership on a business that is not a demo never shows it.
7. **Where the marker sits (CI on PR #74).** As a chip after the name inside the switcher it squeezed "Northwind Skincare" to 52 of its 67 px at 1280 and 1440 px (no-clip, shell-chrome) and its tooltip replaced "Switch business" on hover (businesses.spec). After the switcher, in the bar's row, it cost the search its whole placeholder at 1440. Chosen: a quiet caption under the name (the name then keeps one line, which both demo names fit), taking no width from the bar, and one tooltip on the trigger: "Switch business" with the caption's line under it. Considered: the marker inside the switcher's popover only (not visible at a glance, which is the point), a shorter chip (still costs the search its placeholder).
8. **The LIVE search flake** (live.spec "Find on Foundmill", failed once, passed on retry with the same data): the spec typed into the server-rendered input before React hydrated it, so the typed text was lost and the search never ran. Nothing in the seed or the roll exposes a demo profile (the CI stack seeds today, so it never rolls). The spec now waits for `settled` before typing, as e2e/helpers.ts asks of every LIVE screen followed by an interaction.


## 2026-11-11 — The thesis, and the founder's first five answers to docs/THESIS_REVIEW.md §14

The founder restated what Foundmill is for (as AI spreads, most people move from being employed to building something of their own, and would rather build with others; founders finding each other and becoming cofounders is the core loop; the user is a young, first-time founder; the worry about work stays subtext, never text) and answered five of the eleven decisions in `docs/THESIS_REVIEW.md` §14. These supersede the Oct 5 "desktop web only" decision (BUILD §19), the Oct 12 seat limits and the "Human team = staff" vocabulary. CLAUDE.md carries each as a rule.

1. **Small screens: yes, for the outer product; the native app takes over on phones.** Public pages, sign-in, the profile, People, messages, Today and Needs you work from 390 px. The dense console stays 1280 px and wider. A mobile app is being built with Newly for the iOS App Store and Google Play; once it launches, the small-screen site sends people to download the app. Consequence for the build: the small-screen web is the bridge until then, so it is built from the existing design system with responsive variants and no separate mobile code base, and the hand-over is one config value (the store links, unset until launch) read by the small-screen shell. **Judgment call, to confirm with the founder before package 2.1:** whether a shared public link (a project page, an invitation, a milestone card) opened on a phone after launch still shows its content with a download prompt, or sends the visitor straight to the store. A link that shows nothing loses its preview and its reader; the roadmap assumes content plus prompt for public pages and a redirect for signed-in surfaces.
2. **People are free: yes.** No limit on cofounders or collaborators on any plan; plans differ by agent work only; cofounders' included credits pool on a shared project. Done in this change: `TIERS[*].seats` is "unlimited" (`tierSeatLimit`), `assertSeatAvailable` refuses nothing by default and keeps its refusal behind `limitFor` as the business limit does. Pooling is package 3.9 and not built yet.
3. **The words for people: yes.** Cofounder, collaborator, People; "project" before a first sale and "business" after; "team" stays the agents; "staff" and "seat" leave default copy. Internal identifiers keep their names. The copy sweep itself is package 3.3 and 6.10; new copy follows the rule from today.
4. **Sign-up: a waitlist ordered by referral now; open sign-up once per-account spend ceilings and reporting of people, profiles and messages exist** (packages 2.2 and 4.7).
5. **Ownership splits: the scope stands, and no lawyer's review is required.** The product records a split the cofounders agree, with vesting in plain words, and gives them a founders' agreement they sign themselves, all inside the platform. Reading with the never-list: an agent still never signs or accepts terms for anyone; each cofounder signs with their own action. Package 3.8.

Still open from §14: the age floor, English only, the management-game grammar, the north star, what happens to leaderboards, negotiation, the import door and the fifteen-channel Board, and platform-paid infrastructure (a sending domain, repositories and hosting for projects). The roadmap does not act on these until answered.

## 2026-11-11 — Phase 0 of the rebuild, first part: the product stops reporting what did not happen

Packages 0.3, 0.4 and 0.6 of `docs/THESIS_REVIEW.md` §13.

- **A mock connector's result says it is simulated (0.3).** The gateway adds `simulated: true` and a `notice` sentence to the result of every call whose connector ran its mock (`apps/gateway/src/pipeline.ts`, `SIMULATED_NOTICE` in `types.ts`); a live call carries neither. House rule 4 gains one sentence: a result marked simulated is not an action. Why a field on the result and not an error: the golden path and every demo run on mocks, so the call has to succeed; what must not survive is the model reading `{ sent: true }` with nothing beside it. **Not done, and why:** returning "not connected" for a business that never connected the integration, outside development and test. Production runs `INTEGRATIONS_MODE=mock` with `APP_ENV=staging` (plan, gap 4), so that change turns most non-code agent work from "simulated" into "blocked" on the live product in one deploy; it should ship with package 0.5 (templates stop being granted tools nothing serves) so the planner stops assigning that work at the same time. The Composer does not see tool results today, so "never narrate a simulated send as sent" is covered by the house rule on the agent's side only; an eval for it needs a live key and is listed under package 1.8.
- **No invented channel results for a person's own business (0.4).** `mockResultsAllowed(appEnv, isDemo)` (`apps/orchestrator/src/activities/channel.ts`): the hash-made impressions, leads, signups and spend are recorded for a sample business anywhere and for any business in development and test, and nowhere else. On staging and production a person's business gets the note "Nothing measured: this channel has no connection that reports results yet" and no metric row and no lead. `APP_ENV` unset still reads as development (SEC-9, package 0.7).
- **Subtext check (0.6).** Three term groups in `scripts/banned-terms.mjs` (a job as the reason, unemployment, AI taking work), phrases only: the single word "job" is ordinary copy ("the agent finished the job", a contact's job title) and stays. The profile's suggestion "Keep my day job for now" is now "Fits around what I already do".

## 2026-11-11 — Solo is complete; the north star; shared links on a phone

Three answers from the founder, the same day as the first five. The first corrects the framing this session had written into CLAUDE.md and `docs/THESIS_REVIEW.md` ("founders finding each other and becoming cofounders is the core loop; every feature is judged by whether it feeds it").

1. **Solo is complete.** The founder: "our main user profile WILL be a solopreneur on their own and we should never make them feel like they need another human, they should feel empowered that they CAN do it all on their own, it's just that many or even most may prefer to find a cofounder or collaborator, and we also must make sure we seamlessly facilitate that." The rule (CLAUDE.md): nothing is gated on a second person; no screen, note, prompt or metric treats one person as missing someone; agents cover every gap by default; finding people is one step away and always an offer. What this changes in the roadmap: "start something" stays the main door with "find a project or people" beside it; People is hidden for a founder building alone who has not asked for it; suggestions of people go only to someone who said they are looking; ideation presents every concept as one the founder can run alone, and a possible partner is one optional line that never lowers a rating; "first cofounder" is a milestone that appears when it happens, never an empty slot; the examples lead with one person. Every package of phases 3 and 4 carries this as a guard. What makes the rule true in practice is phase 1 (real outcomes), so it stays ahead of the people work.
2. **North star: weekly active projects with at least one real outcome that week**, whatever the number of people. Not "two or more people", which this session had proposed. "Real outcome" is defined as a real outside action or result (a page live, an email sent, a post published, a payment taken, a customer's reply); simulated results never count. **Judgment calls:** the share of projects with two or more people is kept as a description of what founders chose and never as a target; the north star is also read for one-person projects alone, so the product notices if it serves them worse. The target figure waits for the first measurements.
3. **Shared links on a phone after the app launches ("for now"):** a public page (a project or person page, an invitation, a milestone card) keeps its content with a prompt to get the app; a signed-in small screen is sent to download it.

With the north star decided, `docs/foundmill-plan/foundmill-plan.md` is rewritten (package 0.2): the vision, for whom, principles, the north star, features, growth, metrics, risks, the known gaps as this review found them, and a roadmap whose Now is the rest of phase 0 and phase 1. Stale facts are corrected (the free tier has credits; code sessions start from the repository; migrations through 0074). It parses with `parsePlanFile` (27 roadmap items, no errors). Putting it into Foundmill's own business (Product › Vision › Import a plan) is the founder's step. Items of the old roadmap whose state this session could not verify (the first live engineering loop, repository context, the deploy connector, reviews, runbooks, the load baseline) are carried as one Later item that says to check each against PROGRESS before planning it.

## 2026-11-11 — The last five answers to docs/THESIS_REVIEW.md §14

Every decision of §14 is now answered. The founder chose, from lettered options:

1. **Age: 18 or older for everything, asked once at sign-up** (not only for the network). Consequence: the attestation moves from the first step onto the network (`network_attestations`, `live.profile.confirmAdult`) to sign-up, and an account that has not confirmed cannot start or join a project. Not built yet (roadmap package 2.2); until it is, only the network asks.
2. **Language: English only for the foreseeable future; no effort on translation.** The US English rule stands as it is. The string catalog and the translation layer (package 7.3) leave the roadmap. More countries for payouts and incorporation stay a later question of their own.
3. **The management-game wording stays the default view** (the Form strip, ratings out of 20, Agent of the week, Hire / Let go / Lend, wage cap, line manager). The Oct 20 decision stands; this session had recommended moving it behind Builder view and the founder declined. Package 6.9 is cut down to what CLAUDE.md already requires: the `package.json` description and any visible football-era word. The visible tab slugs keep their names.
4. **What does not serve the thesis.**
   - **Leaderboards: removed** (routes, widgets, the status strip tile, the api procedures, the tables). In progress in its own change.
   - **Agent-to-agent negotiation: left dormant and hidden.** Nothing is built or removed.
   - **The import door: kept as a smaller third option** after "start something" and "find a project or people" (package 2.4 / 2.7).
   - **The Channel Board shows only channels that can really act;** the rest are listed as not available yet in Builder view. This is the visible half of package 0.5.
5. **What Foundmill pays for.**
   - **Project pages at a Foundmill address, hosted by Foundmill, free: yes.** Unblocks packages 1.1 to 1.3.
   - **A Foundmill-provided address a project can send email from: yes, with a daily cap per project and the existing approval for outside contact.** Unblocks package 1.5. **Judgment call to confirm when it is built:** the size of the cap (the roadmap assumes a small default, of the order of tens of messages a day, raised per project by the founder of Foundmill, never by an agent).
   - **Foundmill-owned repositories and hosting for software projects: yes, after pages and email are proven.** Package 1.7 stays last in phase 1.

## 2026-11-11 — Security prerequisites (package 0.7): what was fixed, and the calls made

`docs/SECURITY_REVIEW.md` "Re-check and fixes, Nov 11, 2026" has the table. Judgment calls:

- **A signature callback that names no contract writes nothing.** Before, an unmatched Dropbox Sign callback was written as `contract.signed` "as the connector reported it". The request id in that callback is the one field the vendor's signature does not cover, so the event log would say a contract was signed on the word of whoever posted it. Now only a contract the business itself tied to the request can be reported signed; a late callback for a terminated contract writes nothing either. An event without a request id that the platform itself wrote passes through as before.
- **The contract lookup takes whole ids of 12 characters or more.** Dropbox Sign ids are 40 hexadecimal characters; 12 is a floor that no real vendor id falls under and that makes guessing pointless. A reference stored as a URL still matches when the id is a whole path segment or query value.
- **`APP_ENV=test` is allowed in a production process.** The stricter rule (only staging or production) would fail CI's own check that the built image boots, which runs it with `APP_ENV=test`. What is refused is the silent default: unset, empty or `development`.
- **The gateway's development secret fallback is left.** Requiring a flag for it breaks `pnpm dev` and the local e2e stack for everyone; with the rule above a production process can no longer be in development by accident, which was the way in.
- **Live webhooks keep no replay guard of their own.** Each connector recorded a delivery inside `verify()`, before ingest had stored anything, so a vendor's retry after a refusal or a failed write was answered 401 for good (found by the new binding test: the same GitHub delivery, refused for the wrong installation and retried for the right one). Ingest already holds a nonce per delivery and releases it on every failure; it is now the only guard. The mocks keep theirs.
- **GitHub deliveries are bound to the installation the gateway verified** (`app_installations`), falling back to the stored account id. A business whose GitHub row has neither is refused. GitHub is the one connector live in production: the connect flow has recorded the verified installation since migration 0015, so connected businesses have it; if production's App posts to a URL without a business, those deliveries were already refused before this change.
- **Not done:** SEC-14 (gateway routes for key validation, registry lookups, connect and health, and taking vendor keys out of the api), N2 and N3 (designed with email and Stripe in phase 1), the vendor confirmation for Dropbox Sign, and N4.

## 2026-11-11 — Leaderboards removed: the calls made

- **The tables are dropped, not kept empty** (migration 0076). The only rows were the sample business's, written by the demo seed; no person's data is lost. Rollout: an api still on the old code would fail on `live.leaderboards.*` between the migration and its own redeploy, which only matters where `LIVE_MODE` is open (production has it closed).
- **Saved table views of the two removed tables are deleted** in the same migration: they have no table to apply to. This is the one deletion beyond the feature's own tables.
- **No redirect from `/leaderboards`.** A removed feature answers not found; nothing linked there from outside the product.
- **Connecting to a business keeps its api path** though no screen offers it now. Person-to-person connections are untouched; the business branch is kept for the project pages of phase 1, where "follow this project" needs it.
- **The mobile prompt pack is now wrong about leaderboards** and should be corrected before the app's network screens are built (package 5.6).

## 2026-11-11 — The Channel Board shows only channels that can act: the calls made

The founder decided the rule (§14 item 9). How it was built, and what follows from it:

- **"Can act" is declared, not inferred.** `CHANNEL_ACTING_TOOLS` names, per channel, the gateway tools that count as the channel reaching people. A channel is available when one of them belongs to a connector that is live on the deployment and connected for the business. Twelve channels have an empty list today; adding a publisher is an edit to that table, pinned by a test.
- **What a real project sees in production today:** no channel. Email's connector is not live; SEO and GEO act through a code repository. The Board says "No channel can run on its own yet. Email opens first, as soon as sending is switched on for your project. Nothing is wrong, and nothing is waiting on you." Until package 1.5 (the Foundmill sending address) lands, distribution for real projects is honestly paused rather than simulated.
- **A repository-backed channel is never named as "what opens first".** The first build said "SEO opens first: connect GitHub" once GitHub was live; a first-time founder is not sent to connect a code repository to find customers. SEO and GEO still appear with their reason under "Not available yet" in Builder view.
- **Offline tracking is the one exception, and it cannot be switched on from the Board.** A channel already in offline tracking (print and broadcast by default, or where the Strategist required it) may be put on the board from Builder view: the team prepares the materials and the founder submits them, which claims nothing false. No setter was added, since letting any channel switch into offline tracking would reopen all fifteen.
- **Stopping an engine ends its open campaigns.** A real business that had "live" campaigns on a channel that cannot act will see them read "ended". That is the truth of what they were.
- **Businesses that already enabled such channels keep the row.** Hidden by default, shown as "On the board, not running" in Builder view, no engine. No backfill.
- **With fewer than two routes left, the Board question is not asked**, because a decision needs two options.
- **"Not connected" at the gateway.** A mock connector that the business never connected now answers `integration_not_connected` for a person's own business on staging and production, before anything runs. An integration "connected" in Settings while the deployment is mock (stored with status `mock`) still runs the mock, and its result says it is simulated. Connectors with no account to connect (web search, the platform's own tools) are not affected; web research stays simulated in production until package 1.4.

## 2026-11-11 — Public project pages (package 1.1): the calls made

- **A page is independent of Personal/Live.** Publishing is the founder's explicit act for that page; the old setting governs the network, not this. Sample businesses cannot publish.
- **Names are off by default.** "Built by …" appears only when the founder turns on "Name the people building it"; one name reads "Built by Maya", never as if someone were missing.
- **No model screens a page before it goes public.** The deterministic checks (markup, email addresses, phone numbers) hold it; a model check would cost money on every publish and fail closed on an outage, which would stop a founder publishing their own words. Revisit if pages are abused.
- **Who edits.** The API lets anyone who can write Product save the draft and change the address while the page is not live; the screen shows the tab read-only to people who cannot publish, so in practice owners and admins edit. Only owners and admins publish, take down, or move a live page.
- **Rate limits are shared ceilings** (600 reads a minute per address, 6,000 across all), because the api sees the web server, not the visitor.
- **The address comes from the business's slug,** with a short suffix when another project holds it; `APP_URL` + `/p/<slug>`. Moving a live page writes `page.unpublished` for the old address and `page.published` for the new.
- **Rendered on every request**, so a page taken down disappears at once rather than at the next rebuild.

## 2026-11-11 — SEC-14: the calls made

- **Mock answers stay in the api.** A call that could reach a vendor in live mode goes through the gateway; a mock answer is computed where it is asked, so development and the golden path need no gateway (the pattern the domain check already used).
- **The founder's Anthropic key check moved whole**, including the one-token model call: it was also a direct vendor call.
- **Registry mode follows `LIVE_CONNECTORS`** in the api as in the gateway. Where the two services disagree, the api refuses a simulated answer rather than show it as real.
- **The gateway writes the account settings with the credential;** the api writes them again with who connected. The rule for what a reconnect keeps (the chosen code repository) now lives in both and must stay the same.
- **Not done: a separate secret for the api's service tokens.** It closes N4 but needs a new variable set on the api and the gateway on Railway at the same deploy; doing it in code alone would break production. Recorded for a change made together with the deploy.

## 2026-11-11 — Packages 1.2 and 1.3: the calls made

- **Sonnet at low effort drafts the page**, not Opus like the other first drafts: the page is short and mostly the founder's own text, it sits on the path the founder waits on, and a failure costs nothing visible because the starter draft is used.
- **No founder name reaches the prompt.** "Built by" comes from the owners' names behind the founder's own switch, so a name in the draft could only leak into "about".
- **A draft with a number the founder did not write is thrown away**, not edited: the starter draft replaces it.
- **The team never writes over the founder:** it writes only when the project has no page row.
- **"Later" answers the note;** Today's quiet line takes over. A snooze would need a new column.

## 2026-11-11 — The Foundmill sending address (package 1.5): the calls made

- **A separate connector, not a mode of `sendgrid`.** On the platform's key, `sendgrid`'s other tools (campaigns, stats, the global suppression list) would act on every project's mail at once. `platform_mail` has one tool, and the gateway, not the agent, sets the sender, the unsubscribe link and the signed custom args.
- **A table with fixed, never-reused local parts.** Replies to old messages must reach the project that sent them, after a page address changes and after a project is deleted.
- **"‹project› via Foundmill"** in the From name, and role words and the product's name refused in addresses, so a project cannot pass as a bank, a support desk or Foundmill.
- **Unverified replies are dropped**, not written as unverified. A reply counts, suppresses or links to a lead only when SPF or DKIM passes for the sender's own domain; anything else could be a forgery that silences a real contact or inflates the north star. Cost: a real customer on a misconfigured mail server is not seen. Revisit if founders report missing replies.
- **The caps fail closed.** Without shared counters (Redis down or absent) sending pauses outside development and test, so one project cannot exceed its cap through several gateway copies.
- **A send that may have gone out keeps its count.** Only a definite refusal from SendGrid gives the count back.
- **The founder's own SendGrid connector no longer falls back to the platform key**, and its health check now uses the founder's stored credential (it had been checking the platform account).

## 2026-11-11 — The Follow form (package 1.6): the calls made

- **Double opt-in.** A signup is pending until the person confirms from their own inbox. Only a confirmed follower counts, shows "Agreed to email", is a real outcome or can be emailed by the team. Without it, anyone could record "consent" for a stranger, and the shared sending domain would carry the cost.
- **The confirmation is the platform's email, not the team's:** it needs no founder approval (the person asked for it), goes from the project's Foundmill address even when the founder has their own SendGrid, and counts against the project's daily cap.
- **Three confirmations a day per address across all projects; one follow a day per page and address.** The build first allowed one a day across all projects, which would have silently dropped a real person following a second project the same day; three keeps a script from turning the form into a mail cannon at one address while letting a person follow a few projects in one sitting. One a day per page and address keeps repeats off the page's budget.
- **No per-visitor limit in the api.** The api sees only the web's addresses, and trusting a forwarded visitor address would need a new shared secret. The per-visitor limit belongs in the Vercel Firewall (a rule on POST `/p/*`), which is a setup step, not code.
- **Followers are named "Follower (ma***@example.com)"**, never by the address they typed, so a crafted address cannot read as an instruction where contact names reach agents.

## 2026-11-11 — Email from Foundmill to a person: the calls made

- **One kind to start, "a decision is waiting",** sent after a day unread, once per note, at most one a day per project and three a day per person. Enough to bring someone back to an answer that blocks their project, not a newsletter.
- **The email shows what that person may see:** the Inbox's Finance and CRM walls apply to the quoted subject, through one shared redaction.
- **No link but the one into the app.** Anything that looks like a link, domain or address in text an agent or a founder wrote is replaced, because the email comes from Foundmill's own authenticated domain and a phishing link there would carry Foundmill's name.
- **Its own ceiling, and project mail first.** Reminders never use the budget project mail and follow confirmations need.
- **Replies to `hello@` reach no one,** and the email says so.

## 2026-11-12 — The person profile (package 2.3): the calls made

- **"Building on my own" is the default and shows nothing.** The public page never says a person works alone; a line about people appears only when they chose one. Nothing on the editor asks about people when "on my own" is chosen.
- **Projects appear on a person's page only where that page names its founders**, a choice the founder already made on the project page.
- **The editor is an account page (`/public-profile`)**, not a Settings tab, because a person may have no project.
- **No draft: fields are live; while public, every save is screened** and a held save changes nothing public. A display name changed in "Your profile" is screened too while anything public shows it.
- **Public views are read through the api only.** Browser keys can no longer list every profile or page.
- **A released profile address stays its owner's for 90 days**, so a shared link never starts showing someone else.
### One due-soon window for filings; one monthly-revenue figure for milestones (2026-11-11)
Two follow-ups from the first-time founder report, under "one number per fact" (Phase 1e; the 2026-11-10 entries above).
1. **Filings due soon: 30 days, everywhere.** The Money area's Company line counted 90 days ("2 filings are due in the next 90 days") while Company › Compliance's tile counted 30 ("Due within 30 days"), so the two could disagree on the same business. Chosen: 30 days, the window of the screen the founder acts on, as one shared constant and classifier in `@foundmill/shared/filings` (`FILINGS_DUE_SOON_DAYS`, `filingWindow`, `filingDaysLeft`, `deadlineWindow`). It is used by the Company line (`companyAreaWords`; `companyFacts` in `@foundmill/modules/areas` classifies each deadline with `deadlineWindow`, which keeps a filing's stored `due`/`overdue` status exactly as the calendar does), Compliance's statuses and its "Due within 30 days" tile (`legal-data` `complianceStatus`/`filingStatus`/`calendarRows`; the old `DUE_WINDOW_DAYS` is gone), the nightly roll that moves a filing to `due` (`repos.legal.rollFilingStatuses`), `repos.legal.upcomingDeadlines`' default (the "Legal deadline" wake event and the Inbox reminders), and the agents' `read_legal` tool, whose deadlines now carry `dueSoon`. Days left are whole calendar days from the business's day (`useToday`, `dayKeyIn`) on both the line and the Compliance screen (the screen used to round up a fraction of a day from now, which could count a date with a later time of day one day further). Not this window: how far ahead a list looks (`legal.compliance.list`'s `days`, the Compliance screen's 365, `read_legal`'s 90-day lookahead `PLATFORM_DEADLINE_DAYS`); those are lookaheads, not "due soon" counts. With nothing inside the window the line still says what is next: "Northwind Ltd: nothing is due in the next 30 days; the next filing is Jan 20." (or "…, and no later filing is on the calendar."); the overdue case is unchanged ("1 filing is overdue, and 2 more are due in the next 30 days."). The Chief of Staff answers, Today and the first-week words count no filings, so nothing else changed.
2. **Revenue milestones are Finance's monthly figure.** The `mrr_*` milestones counted payments in the payment's currency over the trailing 30 days (`paymentCountersBefore` with `mrrSince`), while Finance shows the P&L revenue of the last 3 full months ÷ 3 ("MRR", or "Average monthly revenue" for a one-off seller). Chosen: one definition in `@foundmill/shared/monthly-revenue` (`MONTHLY_REVENUE_MONTHS`, `monthlyRevenueWindow`, `monthlyRevenueMinor`, `monthlyRevenueFromCategories`, `monthlyRevenueWords`), with the chart of accounts and `pnlTotals` moved to `@foundmill/shared/pnl` (packages/modules `finance/pnl` re-exports it), so the server adds revenue up with the screens' own function. Used by: the finance router's window (`finance.overview`), Finance › Overview's tile and its `finance.pnl` range, Runway's seeds, the Inbox status strip (`statusStripTiles`, `ReadingPane`'s range), the milestones (`awardRevenueMilestones` and `monthlyRevenueAt` in packages/db `milestones.ts`, over `repos.finance.categoryTotals`, the sums `finance.pnl` returns, and the new `monthlyCategoryTotals` for the month-by-month replay), the demo seed and the MRR in Ledgerly's seeded notes. Same window (UTC months, never the current partial month), same currency rule (the money Finance shows: the first bank currency, else the business's), same function. The figure moves only when a month ends (or a past month's transactions arrive), so a revenue milestone is reached on the first day of the month whose window first passes its threshold; the nightly catch-up (`catchUpMilestones`) awards it, and a payment's own award (`awardMilestonesForConversion`, ingest and the journey build) now counts only customers and enterprise contracts. Its `ref` carries `{ value, threshold, estimate: true, basis: "last_3_full_months_revenue_div_3", months: 3, recurring, amountMinor, currency }` (no month keys: refs do not roll forward with the sample). **Naming:** "MRR" only when revenue recurs (`revenueIsRecurring`, or before an archetype), else "monthly revenue": "First £10K monthly revenue", described "Average monthly revenue reaches £10K: the last 3 full months' revenue ÷ 3, as on Finance (Estimate)." (`TROPHIES[].label(currency, recurring)`, `detectTrophies({ recurring })`, the Milestones screen's and the widget's cards); the Inbox strip's tile reads "Monthly revenue" for a one-off seller; the MRR term's long definition says how the figure is worked out. A revenue milestone has no single win to replay, so opening it says where its figure comes from.
3. **Already-awarded milestones are not rewritten.** Rows awarded under the trailing-30-day rule (`ref.basis: "trailing_30_day_revenue"`) keep their date, label and ref; a trophy is awarded once per business, so the new rule applies only to revenue trophies not yet held. A business that holds "First £1K MRR" from the old rule keeps it even if Finance's figure has not reached £1K yet. The cabinet names cards from the definition, so an old row's card reads in the new words while its stored label stays (cards match rows by `ref.trophyKey`, and by kind plus either label for a row without a key). Considered: recomputing every revenue milestone (rewrites history the founder has seen and shared), dual labels (two facts again).
4. **The seed.** The generator's own revenue trophies (its subscription ledger, or trailing-30-day sales) are no longer stored; after the bank is seeded each sample business gets its revenue milestones from `awardRevenueMilestones` (`seedRevenueMilestones`), so they are reached exactly when Finance's figure first passes each line (roll-forward.test "the seed agrees with itself"); the Inbox milestone note names the latest milestone in the cabinet, and Ledgerly's notes quote Finance's figure ("MRR £… (Estimate)", source "Money received, last 3 full months"). Known limit: the roll-forward moves timestamps by whole days, so after a roll a seeded revenue milestone sits that many days after a month's first day (the bank rows move with it).

### The mobile app follows the web's founder surface, one help control, and the Nov 11 words for people (2026-11-11)
A step back across the active sessions before the next Newly prompt. Judgment calls:
- **The Business tab regroups into the founder's areas** (Plan, Customers, Money, Product; Team is already a tab), with the same module-to-area mapping as `packages/shared/src/areas.ts`, computed on the device from `department.list`. The department card grid built in prompt 11 goes; the department and module screens stay and are reached through their area. Considered: keeping departments as the phone's top level. Rejected: the founder decided on Nov 9 that the sidebar follows the founder's questions, and two clients that disagree on where a tab lives would make the deep links and the Chief of Staff's "Why am I seeing this?" answers inconsistent.
- **The opening sentences are read, never recomputed.** The web builds them in the browser from the tiles' reads; the phone gets them from one new procedure, `business.areas.words` (BACKEND_CHANGES item 8), because six fact-gathering paths copied into a second client would drift from the web's words. Until it ships, the card shows a skeleton, never a sentence of the app's own.
- **One help control on the phone: the Chief of Staff.** The web has "Explain this page" (a screen's three registered lines) and "Ask <Chief of Staff>" side by side. The phone folds the first into the Chief of Staff's "Why am I seeing this?" (the server already falls back to the page kind's purpose when the client sends none), so a small screen never carries two help controls, and the screen's own purpose line travels as `page.purpose`.
- **Builder view never appears on the phone.** The phone is a founder surface; the engine's words (cycle numbers, tokens, test counts, PR numbers, code, health and hierarchy tabs) stay on the web behind the switch. `plainHeadline`, never `headline`.
- **The Nov 11 vocabulary applies to the app now**, before the web is renamed: People, cofounders, collaborators, "project" until the first sale, the subtext rule. A fresh client is cheaper to start right than to rename later; the API's role enum and the `staff` slug are internal and stay. The project word is one helper reading `journey.ribbon().customers.total`, and API-written text is left as it comes.
- **No streaks of any kind on the phone.** Prompt 15's "Answered within a day, N days running" is dropped on the retention review's reasoning (a daily count punishes the vacation the product offers). "Since you were last here" takes its place and is computed from data the API already returns.
- **LIVE social (prompt 16) is on hold and Agent of the week is not built**, because the thesis review plans to retire game mode and rework leaderboards (packages 2.5, 4.9) and asks the founder whether the ratings grammar stays (§14 q7). Building against an API about to change would be waste; the People surfaces that replace them are Phase B.
- **Phase B waits for the backend.** Person profiles, project pages, invitations, join requests, messages and browse are prompts 18–22, each naming its THESIS_REVIEW package; nothing in the app may pretend they exist before the procedures do.

### The mobile pack is corrected before new screens are built (2026-11-11, later)
- **One course-correction prompt (12A) before 12B**, rather than folding the corrections into the next build prompt. Newly keeps a running mental model of the product from its earlier chats; a replaced AGENTS.md, re-read skills and an Ask-mode audit that lists every disagreement with file and line gives the agent one place to learn what changed (solo is complete, People, round of work, Sample business, leaderboards gone, the corrected shapes) and leaves one small Build turn to apply it. Building the areas on top of a stale model would have mixed the two.
- **Leaderboards are deleted from the app, not hidden.** The feature is removed from the product (migration 0076 on the rebuild branch); a hidden screen would be code that calls procedures about to answer not found. Network and Communities are hidden behind one flag because their API changes but survives.
- **The solo framing corrects this morning's words.** The product skill, AGENTS.md and the voice skill said "with other people" and "the founders, never assume one"; the founder's correction (CLAUDE.md "Solo is complete") makes one person complete and the people side an offer. Phase B carries the R0 guard (no empty People panel, no invite step, no suggestion to someone who did not ask).

### The mobile pack follows the afternoon's work in the other sessions (2026-11-11, later)
- **The daily streak replaces "no streaks".** The founder chose a daily streak in the retention session. The phone shows only the server's count (never its own), so a vacation is treated as the server decides and the phone and the web agree; one line in words, no flame or badge.
- **New surfaces wait behind gates, not guesses.** Public pages (12C) wait for package 1.1 on main; Google, Apple, 18 or older and the waitlist (13B) wait for the api to accept them (BACKEND_CHANGES item 12). The backend skill lists their shapes under "Coming" so Newly can read them defensively without building early.
- **Universal links move into prompt 13's native batch.** Founder email with one-click answers will open on phones; adding associated domains with push avoids a second native rebuild.
- **Apple on iOS is flagged for the founder.** App Store Guideline 4.8 is likely to require Sign in with Apple once Google is offered; the backend item records the choice as open.

### The Sample business marker only for visitors exploring the example (2026-11-12)
- **Founder's call:** the "Sample business" caption shows only to a visitor exploring the example (an example-source membership on a demo business), with its line "Its figures are made up for the example: nothing here is real money or real customers. Nothing you do here is saved." The demo's own owner and invited members no longer see it (this supersedes "whoever opens the business" in the Nov 10 entry). `sampleMarker` in `apps/web/lib/access.ts` returns null unless `isDemo` and `isExample` are both true; it still never shows on a founder's own business.

## 2026-11-12 — Google sign-in on the web (package 2.2): the calls made

- **Founder's decision:** Google on the web only, for now; no Apple; the phone app keeps the email code. Relayed by the alignment session and confirmed by the founder in this session before merge.
- **Ownership (founder, Nov 12, relayed by the alignment session):** the network session (`docs/NETWORK.md`) owns invitations (THESIS 3.1) and the referral-ordered waitlist (the waitlist half of 2.2); this roadmap keeps Google sign-in, 2.1 (small screens) and 2.4 (a home for a person with no project).
- **What counts as confirmed:** `amr` oauth plus Google as the account's only social provider plus Google's verified email. `user_metadata.email_verified` is user-writable and can only refuse; the protection is Supabase's own rule (it links and confirms only verified addresses) with "Confirm email" on, which the api now checks itself and fails closed on.
- **Hidden until set up:** the button needs the Vercel flag and a live check of Supabase's settings, so a half-finished setup never shows a button that fails.
- **Not on previews:** the PKCE cookie lives on the preview host and the callback on the apex, so Google stays off on Vercel previews.
- **Password sessions are refused** in the api: the product never issues them, and Supabase has no switch that closes password sign-up alone.
## 2026-11-12 — Team call speech (ElevenLabs connector, gateway routes, api client): the calls made

- **Names.** The thing an agent sounds like is a speech accent (`SPEECH_ACCENTS` in packages/shared): tool field `speechAccent`, gateway field `speechAccent`, error `unknown_speech_accent`, map `elevenlabs/speech-accents.ts` (`SPEECH_ACCENT_IDS`, `speechAccentId`), env `ELEVENLABS_SPEECH_ACCENT_IDS`. ElevenLabs' `voice_id` appears only in that connector. A bare `accent` identifier is avoided because the design system uses it for its accent color.
- **Output format `mp3_44100_64`** (MP3, 44.1 kHz, 64 kbps), not the default 128 kbps: clear speech at half the bytes, and the api carries each turn as base64. Flash v2.5 and Scribe v3 come from `SPEECH_MODELS`; nothing here names a model.
- **Cost Estimates, per character.** Synthesis is `ceil(characters x 0.005)` US cents, at least 1 (Flash v2.5 about $0.05 per 1,000 characters on the API plans, ElevenLabs' pricing page read Nov 12, 2026). Transcription cannot know the audio's length before the call, so it takes the recording's size at about 16 KB a second against Scribe v3's about $0.40 an hour, at least 1 cent. Both are constants in `elevenlabs/tools.ts`, labeled Estimate in the audit row and the usage row. The ledger row is a `tool` row in USD (the vendor's currency), not the business's, and `tool` rows are never charged to credits (`chargesCredits`), so this is a record, not a charge.
- **The daily ceiling is characters, not calls.** `sp:<businessId>:day` is an INCRBY of the text's length in a UTC-day window, ceiling `TEAM_CALL_LIMITS.speechCharsPerBusinessPerDay` (60,000, about 200 turns). To count by more than one, `RateLimitStore.hit` and `release` take an optional amount (`RedisCounter` gains optional `incrby` and `decrby`; ioredis has both). The count is taken before the call and given back when the call was refused after counting, when the key is missing, and when the vendor refused outright (a 4xx other than a 408: nothing was spoken). After a timeout or a 5xx the audio may have been made and billed, so it stays counted, the same rule as the sending address.
- **The durable-store rule applies, to live calls only.** A cap other replicas cannot see caps nothing, so outside development and test a live synthesis is refused with 503 `speech_store_unavailable` while the count is not shared (no Redis, or Redis down). The mock costs nothing and counts in memory anywhere. Transcription does not count against the character ceiling (it is the founder's own speech, bounded by 8 MiB and the connector's window).
- **Order of the checks** is the contract's: accent, words, business, window, day, call. The business is read between the words and the window so a made-up id never creates counters.
- **Failures reach the api as a code, a status and a fixed sentence**, never the vendor's words: 422 `speech_refused` (the request will not succeed), 429 `speech_rate_limited`, 502 `speech_unavailable` (including a refused platform key, which is the gateway's configuration), 503 `speech_unconfigured`.
- **The mock carries `simulated: true` in its data;** the gateway adds `simulated` and the usual `SIMULATED_NOTICE` to its own answer when it ran the mock. The api's in-process mock answers `simulated: true` and the call shows `TEAM_CALL_WORDS.simulatedNotice` once (`speechModes`).
- **Accent overrides are config, not secrets,** but travel the same way as the key (`PlatformSecrets.elevenlabsSpeechAccentIds`). Parsed once when the connector is built; an unknown accent, a malformed id (ids go into a URL path, so only 10 to 40 letters and digits pass) or bad JSON is ignored with a warning logged once, never echoing a value.
- **Multipart in the shared HTTP client.** `createHttp` now hands a `FormData` body to fetch untouched (and drops any content-type so fetch writes the boundary); every other body behaves as before.
- **`ApiGatewayClient` gains two required methods** (`synthesizeSpeech`, `transcribeSpeech`), so the six test doubles that implement the whole interface each gained two stubs.

## 2026-11-12 — Team call data (packages/db, migration 0082): the calls made

- **Read gating follows the agent desk: plain member read.** `agent_threads` and `agent_messages` (0020) use `fm_tenant_table` standard: every member reads, owners, admins and members write, and no SQL policy looks at sections; the desk's Team wall is the api's. The three Team call tables do the same (`team_calls_select`, `team_call_turns_select`, `team_call_actions_select` are `is_member(business_id)`); the api's `businessProcedure` with Team read and Team write is the section wall. Viewers, example viewers (always viewers, 0065) and advisors read only, since every write policy is `has_role(owner, admin, member)`.
- **Only a call changes after insert, and only in the columns the lifecycle needs.** Turns and action items take `fm_tenant_table(..., 'read_only')` plus an insert policy for owner, admin and member, and `authenticated` loses update and delete on them (the generated suite runs them as `append`). On `team_calls` the standard write policy stays and `authenticated` keeps update only on `status, summary, agent_rounds_since_founder, turn_count, ended_at, updated_at` (column grants); kind, agents, topic, starter, start time and business never change. No app role deletes a call (no delete grant): a call goes with its business. Service code keeps every privilege.
- **Turns and action items carry `updated_at` too.** TEAM_CALL.md §2 lists only `created_at` for them, but every table but `audit_log` has `updated_at` with its trigger (BUILD §7, the structural test in `migrations.test.ts`). App roles cannot update the rows, so it equals `created_at` for everything the api writes.
- **"An agent turn has `agent_id`, a founder turn has `user_id`" is a trigger on insert, not a check.** Both columns are `on delete set null`; a check would refuse the null a deleted agent or account leaves and so block that delete. The checks that stay (`team_call_turns_agent_shape`, `team_call_turns_founder_shape`) only say what a turn never carries (an agent turn no person and no `via`, a founder turn no agent), which survives the set null; `fm_team_call_turns_speaker` (23514) requires the speaker on insert. Tests delete an agent, an account and a business with turns.
- **The calls a repo cannot leave to a foreign key.** A turn or action item points at its call through `(call_id, business_id)` (the 0020 pattern), so it never sits on another business's call. `agent_ids` is an array (no foreign key): `repos.teamCalls.create` checks every id is an agent of the business (`assertAgentsOwned`), and `finish` checks the agents, departments, plans and plan actions it records. `appendTurns` also refuses an agent turn by, or addressed to, an agent not on the call.
- **`appendTurns` also enforces the agent-round limit under its lock.** An agent-only round (`{ agentRound: true }`) is refused (ValidationError) once `agent_rounds_since_founder` reached `TEAM_CALL_LIMITS.agentRoundsWithoutFounder`, so two "Let the team discuss" rounds at once cannot both pass the api's check. A retried founder turn (same `client_turn_id`) returns the recorded turn even when the call is no longer live; a new turn on a call that is not live is refused.
- **`plans.origin` widened by replacing `plans_origin_check`** (0005's inline check) with the same list plus `team_call`; nothing else in the plans policies changed.
- **The speech accent: the default is derived, only the founder's override is stored.** `agents.speech_accent` (nullable, `^[a-z]{2,20}$`) holds a founder's own choice; null means the default from `defaultSpeechAccents` in packages/shared, computed on every read and never written. The api checks the key is one of `SPEECH_ACCENTS`; the database checks only its shape. Choosing it is an owner's or an admin's: the agents write policy (0004) lets members write every column and RLS cannot narrow one column per role, so the guard trigger `fm_agents_speech_accent_guard` refuses an app role that sets or changes it unless `has_role(owner, admin)` (42501, "permission denied"); the 0004 policy itself is unchanged and members still edit every other column. `repos.agents.setSpeechAccent(db, businessId, agentId, key | null)`. Never a bare `accent` identifier (it clashes with the design system's accent color).
- **Numbered 0082** (journal idx 70): first planned as 0082 behind another branch's 0082 and 0083, then renumbered when Team call merged first (founder, Nov 12, 2026); that branch follows it.

## 2026-11-12 — Team call turn director and wrap-up (packages/models, packages/agents, evals): the calls made

- **The calls send loose drafts; the finalisers return the §4 shapes.** `buildTeamCallTurnRequest` and `buildTeamCallWrapUpRequest` send `TeamCallTurnsDraft` / `TeamCallWrapUpDraft` (no length limits, which structured outputs do not enforce anyway), so a 610-character turn or a 21st action is repaired locally instead of costing a repair call while the founder waits, as the desk conversation does with `AgentReplyDraft`. `finaliseTeamCallTurns` and `finaliseTeamCallWrapUp` always return values within `TeamCallTurnsOutput` / `TeamCallWrapUpOutput` (tests parse them). The contract's `PlanActionSchema` is the existing `PlanAction`.
- **Finalised shapes are camel at the top, the plan action untouched.** A turn is `{ agentId, to, text }` with `to` the shared `TeamCallAddressee` (`"founder" | "team" | { agentId }`, what `team_call_turns.addressed_to` stores); an action item is `{ title, detail, agentId, action }` with `action` exactly as the compiler writes it.
- **Mentions are stored with the stored name.** The prompt shows the starter's display names; `finaliseTeamCallTurns` rewrites every valid `[[ent:<id>|…]]` to the participant's stored name (`name`, default `displayName`), so stored text is the same for every reader and each listener's rules apply when it is spoken (§1).
